### 2026-07-01 — Fix: sidebar collapse animation, a divider under the toggle, and a re-drawn toggle icon

- Fixed/Built: The sidebar's collapse/expand width change now actually animates. Root cause: `Sidebar.tsx` toggled a `.gw-sidebar--animated` class from a `useEffect` keyed on `[collapsed]`, which commits one render AFTER the `width` prop already changed — by the time the transition-bearing class arrived, the browser had already snapped to the new width with nothing left to interpolate, so `434a54d`'s "Polish sidebar collapse control" had shipped a transition that never visibly ran. Replaced the effect with a synchronous-during-render check (`prevCollapsedRef` compared and updated inline, no `useEffect`) so the `--animated` class lands in the **same commit** as the width change — exactly the commit a CSS transition needs to see an old→new value change while the rule is active. Verified empirically: sampling the sidebar's `getBoundingClientRect().width` 80ms into the 200ms transition now reads ~81px between the 52px (collapsed) and 180px (expanded) extremes, where it previously read the final value instantly. Also added a 1px divider (reusing the same style already used between nav groups) directly under the collapse-toggle row, in both collapsed and expanded layouts, separating the toggle from the nav list below it the same way groups are already separated from each other. Replaced the `◫` glyph in the toggle button with a custom inline SVG (rounded-rect outline + a vertical divider line drawn at x=6.5 of 16, left of the rect's x=8 center) — a clearer "toggle sidebar" pictogram, and the asked-for "stick shifted slightly left" is now an explicit coordinate instead of an uncontrollable glyph-rendering detail.
- Regression caught before commit: a first attempt made the width/flex-basis transition unconditional on `.gw-sidebar` (always on) and tried to suppress it only via a `resizing` prop during an active resize-handle drag. `npx playwright test shell.spec.ts` then hung and timed out on "side panels resize, clamp, and restore persisted widths" — bisected with `git stash` against the prior commit (passed) vs. with the change (failed reproducibly), then root-caused with a throwaway debug spec: immediately after a drag's `mouseup`, `getBoundingClientRect().width` read 241 while the just-written `localStorage` value (the React state's logical target) already read 250 — the final width-settling commit and the `resizing→false` commit (which re-enables the transition) landed close enough together that the last few pixels of the drag's own width-snap got caught mid-transition, so the test's `waitForFunction(stored.left === capturedLeft)` compared a transient DOM value against a value that storage would never produce again. The `collapsed`-gated approach above sidesteps the whole class of bug: dragging the resize handle never flips `collapsed`, so the animated class is structurally never present during a drag — no prop, no timing race. The now-dead `resizing` prop was removed from `Sidebar`'s interface and from its `App.tsx` call site.
- Files: edited `src/renderer/components/Sidebar.tsx`, `src/renderer/theme.css`, `src/renderer/App.tsx` (removed the `resizing` prop pass-through; `resizingPanel` state itself is still used by `PanelResizeHandle`).
- Tests: `shell.spec.ts`'s "side panels resize, clamp, and restore persisted widths" — failed reproducibly with the rejected approach, passed 3/3 with the fix. Full `shell.spec.ts` + `tooltip.spec.ts` together: **9/9 passed**, including the sidebar-collapse tooltip test. `electron-vite build` clean; ESLint clean; Prettier clean on all three touched files.
- Notes / follow-ups: Not a numbered phase. No `src/core`/`src/main`/IPC changes.

### 2026-07-01 — Fix: sidebar collapse animation never ran in `npm run dev` (StrictMode)

- Fixed/Built: The prior fix above (`6d0bc12`) still didn't animate in development. Root cause: `Sidebar.tsx` derived the `.gw-sidebar--animated` class by **mutating a ref during render** (`prevCollapsedRef.current !== collapsed`, then `prevCollapsedRef.current = collapsed`) — an impure render. `main.tsx` wraps the app in `React.StrictMode`, which **double-invokes render functions in development**: the first invocation saw the flip and set the ref, so the second invocation (whose output React actually commits) saw `prev === collapsed` and emitted **no** animated class. The class therefore never reached the committed DOM under `npm run dev`, so the width snapped. It worked under `npm run e2e` only because that runs the **production** build (`out/main/index.js`), where StrictMode does not double-invoke — which is exactly why `6d0bc12`'s "verified empirically" passed while the dev app a developer actually uses stayed broken. Fix: removed the render-time ref derivation and instead drive the class from real `animating` state set inside the collapse toggle's **click handler** (`handleToggleCollapse`), cleared by a `SIDEBAR_TRANSITION_MS` timer (cleanup on unmount). Event handlers run once (StrictMode never double-invokes them) and the `setAnimating(true)` batches with the parent's `onToggleCollapse` collapse flip into a single commit, so the animated class and the new `width`/`flex-basis` land together — the precise condition a CSS transition needs — identically in dev and prod. Resize-drag still never flips `collapsed` and never calls the toggle, so `animating` stays false throughout a drag: the same structural guarantee against the drag-race regression `6d0bc12` documented, now independent of render purity. Updated the explanatory comment in `theme.css` to match.
- Files: edited `src/renderer/components/Sidebar.tsx` (replaced the impure `prevCollapsedRef` derivation with `animating` state + a click-handler-driven timer), `src/renderer/theme.css` (comment only).
- Tests: Vitest **720/720 passed**. `electron-vite build` clean (both `tsc` projects). `shell.spec.ts` + `tooltip.spec.ts` in real Electron: **9/9 passed** — the resize test that the rejected `resizing`-prop approach once hung still passes, and the sidebar-collapse tooltip test still passes (no functional regression). ESLint clean; Prettier clean on both touched files. The animation itself is a development-only StrictMode behavior the production-build e2e cannot observe by construction; verified by deterministic root-cause analysis plus the fix making the dev render path identical to the prod path e2e already exercises.
- Notes / follow-ups: Not a numbered phase. No `src/core`/`src/main`/IPC changes, so core-purity/safety reviewers were not required. Lesson: deriving render output by mutating a ref during render is not StrictMode-safe — prefer event-handler state for "did the user just do X" signals.

### 2026-07-02 — Phase 69: GitRunner conflict classification + local merge (main)

- Built: Fixed the stdout-classification blind spot — `GitRunner.execute()` previously classified only `stderr` on a non-zero exit, but a real `git merge` conflict writes `CONFLICT (…)` to **stdout** with empty stderr, so it fell through to `unknown`. Now `ErrorMapper.map()` is called against `[stderr, stdoutText].filter(Boolean).join('\n')` — both streams combined for classification — while the existing secret-safe `console.error` diagnostic log (stderr-only) is unchanged. Added `GitService.mergeRemoteBranch(repoPath, remote, branch)`: runs `git merge --no-edit <remote>/<branch>` with **no `auth`/`extraEnv`** — it integrates the already-fetched local remote-tracking ref, so it is purely local (no network call, no credential env), matching the plan's "local merge only" boundary. `ErrorMapper`'s existing `/CONFLICT \(/` matcher needed no changes — the fix was entirely in `GitRunner` feeding it the right text.
- Files: edited `src/main/git/GitRunner.ts`, `src/main/services/GitService.ts`, `tests/integration/git-service.test.ts` (+2 new tests).
- Tests: Vitest **725/725 passed** (2 new integration tests against real offline temp repos with a local bare-repo remote: (1) "merges cleanly when the diverged changes do not conflict" — local commits an unrelated file, remote pushes to `base.txt`, `mergeRemoteBranch` resolves and produces a real 2-parent merge commit with both files' content present; (2) "rejects a merge as `mergeConflict` on a real content conflict, leaving the repo mid-merge" — same line of `base.txt` edited on both sides, `mergeRemoteBranch` rejects with `GitError.code === 'mergeConflict'` **despite empty stderr** (confirmed in the test's captured `[GitRunner]` log line), `.git/MERGE_HEAD` exists, and `getStatus` reports the file `conflicted` on both index/worktree — the regression proof for the stdout fix). `npx tsc --noEmit` clean on both tsconfigs. `npm run lint`: ESLint clean repo-wide; Prettier clean on every touched file (same 7-file pre-existing/unrelated debt untouched, per the established precedent).
- Exit criteria: ✅ met — safety-reviewer confirmed args stay an array, `GitRunner` remains the only spawn caller, no secret/credential material can leak via the combined stdout+stderr classification text (the token only ever reaches git via `GIT_ASKPASS` env, never argv/stdout/stderr), and `mergeRemoteBranch` sets no credential env / makes no network call. No UI.
- Notes / follow-ups: Phase 68's `remediationExecutor.ts` stub case (`merge-remote-into-local` → `{ ok: false, message: 'Merging in the remote changes is not yet available.' }`) is now replaced by real logic in Phase 70, which wires `mergeRemoteBranch` + a clean-tree pre-check behind the `remediation:execute` IPC channel.

### 2026-07-02 — Phase 70: Executable merge remediation (IPC)

- Built: `'merge-remote-into-local'` added to `RemediationExecutePayload.action`'s Zod enum (`ipc-schemas.ts`). Phase 68's stub case in `remediationExecutor.ts` replaced with the real executor: requires `input.branch` (refuses with a clear message if missing); `remote` defaults to `'origin'`; a **clean-tree pre-check** via `getStatus` refuses with "Commit or stash your changes before merging in the remote's changes." _without_ attempting the merge when `status.files.length > 0`; otherwise calls `mergeRemoteBranch(repoPath, remote, branch)` and returns `{ ok: true }` on success. Catches a `GitError` with `code === 'mergeConflict'` and returns `{ ok: false, remediation: remediationForGitError('mergeConflict'), message: error.userMessage }` — the repo is left in git's standard mid-merge state, never auto-resolved; any other error re-throws so `wrap()` classifies it through the structured IPC envelope. `RemediationExecutorDeps.git` extended from `Pick<GitService, 'setLocalIdentity' | 'push' | 'getRemotes'>` to also include `'getStatus' | 'mergeRemoteBranch'`.
- Files: edited `src/main/ipc/ipc-schemas.ts`, `src/main/ipc/remediationExecutor.ts`, `tests/unit/remediation-actions.test.ts` (+4 new tests, plus `getStatus`/`mergeRemoteBranch` added to the file's existing mock `deps.git` fixtures so the widened `RemediationExecutorDeps.git` type still compiles).
- Tests: Vitest **729/729 passed** (4 new in `remediation-actions.test.ts`, offline fixtures with a local bare-repo remote: (1) a clean divergent merge → `ok: true`, a real 2-parent merge commit exists; (2) a conflicting merge → `ok: false`, `remediation` exactly `{ action: 'resolve-conflicts', kind: 'navigate', navigateTo: 'status' }`, repo left mid-merge (`getStatus` reports `base.txt` `conflicted` on both index/worktree); (3) a dirty working tree → the pre-check refusal fires and `mergeRemoteBranch` is asserted **never called**; (4) no `branch` provided → refuses with a branch-specific message). `npx tsc --noEmit` clean on both tsconfigs. `npm run lint`: ESLint clean repo-wide; Prettier clean on every touched file (same 7-file pre-existing/unrelated debt untouched, per the established precedent).
- Exit criteria: ✅ met — safety-reviewer confirmed no secrets logged anywhere in the diff; the action is genuinely local/network-free (`mergeRemoteBranch` takes no `auth`/credential env, contrasted directly against `switch-profile-and-retry-push`'s askpass-env path in the same switch); the dirty-tree pre-check runs before any merge attempt on every code path; a real conflict is never auto-resolved (no `merge --abort`, no forced commit); the button click triggers exactly the merge and nothing else (no auto-fetch, no auto-push, no profile/settings mutation, unlike the adjacent `switch-profile-and-retry-push` case). No UI.
- Notes / follow-ups: Logic-complete checkpoint for Diverged-Branch Merge (Phases 68–70 all green Vitest, no UI yet). Phase 71 is next and is the feature-complete stop point: wire `RemediationButton`'s executable switch + label for `merge-remote-into-local`, source the recovery banner's `remote`/`branch` from `remoteStore.lastFailure` (currently push-only via `selectedRemote`), and cover with Playwright e2e (clean merge → banner clears → Push succeeds; conflicting merge → "Go to Status" link → file shown unmerged; dirty tree → refusal, if cheap).

### 2026-07-02 — Phase 71: Merge button in the failed-pull recovery banner (renderer + e2e)

- Built: **Feature-complete stop point for Diverged-Branch Merge.** `doPull` (`remoteStore.ts`) now populates a structured `lastFailure` on a failed pull (`{ message, code, remediation, remote, branch }`) instead of discarding the IPC envelope's structured fields — mirroring `doRemotePush`, and clearing `lastFailure` at the start of every pull. The `lastFailure` type gained `remote?`/`branch?` because the recovery banner's push path previously read those only from `selectedRemote`, which is `null` on a pull failure. **RemoteScreen**: the existing failed-push recovery banner now also renders for a failed pull — the `RemediationButton`'s `remote`/`branch` source from `lastFailure.remote ?? selectedRemote?.name` / `lastFailure.branch ?? currentBranch`, so a `divergentBranches` pull failure produces a working merge button with no other banner changes. **RemediationButton**: added the `'merge-remote-into-local'` case to the executable label switch (`STR.REMEDIATION_MERGE_REMOTE(remote, branch)` → "Bring in origin/main", falling back to a generic label when either is missing) plus a `merge-remote-into-local` local-only hint line; the `missingTarget` guard now also requires `remote` + `branch` for this action, disabling the button rather than sending an incomplete request. A real conflict's re-diagnosis (the executor's `resolve-conflicts` remediation) flows through the existing `onFailure` → `setLastFailure` wiring unchanged, re-rendering the banner as a "Go to Status" navigate link — no new conflict-resolution UI. A clean merge's `onSuccess` clears messages and reloads; the user clicks the existing Push button separately (no auto-push, no auto-fetch). All new copy externalized in `strings.ts` (`REMEDIATION_MERGE_REMOTE`, `REMEDIATION_MERGE_REMOTE_GENERIC`, `REMEDIATION_MERGE_LOCAL_ONLY_HINT`).

### 2026-07-02 — Fix: Screens went stale on an in-place branch switch (History, Status, Commit, Remote, Safety Center)

- Fixed: Reported by the user — switching the active branch via the always-mounted `GlobalHeader` branch dropdown (or `BranchesScreen`) updates `appStore.currentBranch` immediately, but five other screens' data-loading `useEffect`s only re-ran on `activeRepo` change, never on a same-repo branch switch. A user sitting on any of these screens while switching branches (a normal flow — the header dropdown never navigates away) kept seeing the **previous branch's** data with no error, no stale indicator. Root cause was identical across all five: `useEffect(() => { ... load(...) }, [activeRepo, load])` — `activeRepo` is the same object reference across a branch switch, so React never re-ran the effect. Fixed by adding the live branch as a dependency, sourced from `useAppStore((s) => s.currentBranch)` (the single app-wide value `branchStore.doSwitch`/`doCreate` update the instant a switch succeeds — already correctly reactive in `GlobalHeader`/`Inspector`/`RemoteScreen`'s branch display): `HistoryScreen.tsx` (commit log), `StatusScreen.tsx` (staged/unstaged/untracked files + the diff panel, which already keyed off `selectedFile` reset in the same effect), `CommitScreen.tsx` (staged-changes summary feeding the commit gate), `RemoteScreen.tsx` (`upstream`, used by the push-safety check's remote-target resolution — already read `currentBranch` for display, just missing it from `load()`'s deps), and `SafetyCenterScreen.tsx` — the most consequential instance, since this screen keeps its **own** `currentBranch` copy inside `safetyCenterStore` (fetched once per `load()`, independent of `appStore`), so the branch-scoped push-policy verdict (`canPush`, `PROTECTED_BRANCH_PUSH`/`BRANCH_NOT_ALLOWED`) stayed pinned to whichever branch was active when the screen last loaded — a user could switch onto a blocked branch and still see a stale "Yes/Allowed" push verdict. Added a `liveCurrentBranch` read from `appStore` purely as an effect trigger (renamed to avoid shadowing the store's own `currentBranch`, which still drives the rendered verdict — both update together from the same fresh `load()` call once retriggered). `BranchesScreen` and `GlobalHeader`'s guard badge were investigated and are **not** bugs: `BranchesScreen` derives its own branch list directly (self-consistent), and the header guard chip is identity/profile-only by design (its own doc comment: "Calls ONLY getEffectiveIdentity ... needs no remotes/status") — confirmed via `resolvePushTarget`'s precedence rules that it is branch-independent.
- Files: edited `src/renderer/screens/HistoryScreen.tsx`, `src/renderer/screens/StatusScreen.tsx`, `src/renderer/screens/CommitScreen.tsx`, `src/renderer/screens/RemoteScreen.tsx`, `src/renderer/screens/SafetyCenterScreen.tsx`; new regression tests in `tests/e2e/history.spec.ts`, `tests/e2e/status.spec.ts`, `tests/e2e/commit.spec.ts`, `tests/e2e/safety-center.spec.ts` (+4 tests, TDD: each watched RED against the pre-fix code, listed in Tests below).
- Tests: Each new e2e test switches branch via the **header dropdown** while remaining on the target screen (no navigation, no reload) and asserts the data reflects the new branch: History (2 branches, differing commit counts — 1 vs 2 commits) confirmed RED (stuck on 1) → GREEN; Status (an untracked file created on disk _after_ the initial load, then a same-repo branch switch) confirmed RED (still "No untracked files") → GREEN; Commit (a file staged on disk after the initial load) confirmed RED (still "No staged changes") → GREEN; Safety Center (`pushPolicy` with `feature-a` allowed / `main` blocked, switching from the allowed to the blocked branch) confirmed RED (`safety-can-push` stuck on "✓ Yes") → GREEN. Full regression run: Playwright **24/24 passed** across `history/status/commit/safety-center/remote/branches/push-policy.spec.ts` (the pre-existing `branches.spec.ts` "updates the Remote screen" test still passes unchanged). Vitest **729/729 passed** (no unit-testable surface touched — pure renderer `useEffect` deps, no store logic changed). `npx tsc --noEmit` clean on both `tsconfig.node.json` and `tsconfig.web.json`. `npm run lint`: ESLint clean repo-wide; Prettier clean on every touched file (same pre-existing, unrelated Prettier debt on `.claude/**` and `tests/unit/error-mapper.test.ts` untouched, per established precedent).
- Exit criteria: ✅ met — all 4 new regression tests pass, full affected-area e2e suite green, no regressions. UI-only diff (no `src/core`/`src/main`/IPC changes), so core-purity/safety reviewers were not required per the maker≠checker rule.
- Notes / follow-ups: Not a numbered phase — a cross-screen consistency bug fix, same precedent as the Phase 68/71/sidebar `Fix:` entries above. **Known, deliberately unfixed edge case**: `RemoteScreen`'s `upstream` staleness can only produce an observably wrong verdict in a narrow multi-remote scenario (two remotes, each branch tracking a different one as upstream, with an owner/repo push-policy mismatch) — `resolvePushTarget` falls back to the sole/preferred remote whenever there's only one remote, so the common single-remote case was never actually wrong. Fixed the dependency array anyway for consistency (same one-line pattern, zero risk), but did not build the elaborate dual-remote fixture needed to exercise it in e2e — disclosed here rather than silently claimed as tested. AI Chat panel is **not** branch- or repo-scoped by design (conversation persists across switches); flagged to the user as a separate design question, out of scope for this fix.

The storefront release was created **published**, not draft, per the plan's "Open questions" lean (no reason to hold as draft — `gitwarden` stays public until Phase 75, so there's no exposure gap). - Notes / follow-ups: **Incident during the local test-publish, caught and fixed same session.** The test ran against the tag `v0.2.0`, which already had a _published_ 11-asset release on `gitwarden-releases` from Phase 72. `electron-builder`'s GitHub publisher (with `releaseType: draft`) recreated that release fresh rather than appending to it: the existing release was replaced by a new **Draft** carrying only the 5 assets a single-OS macOS build produces (`arm64.dmg`, `x64.dmg` + blockmaps + `latest-mac.yml`), silently dropping the Windows/Linux installers and un-publishing the storefront's live v0.2.0. Recovered by `gh release delete v0.2.0 -R shchadyloTaras/gitwarden-releases --yes --cleanup-tag` followed by a clean re-run of `scripts/migrate-release.sh v0.2.0`; verified afterward — 11 assets restored, release published (not draft), all 5 installer URLs re-confirmed HTTP 200. **Lesson for any future local publish test:** electron-builder's `--publish always` against an _existing_ tag is not additive — it can replace/downgrade a real release. A future ad-hoc cross-repo test should use a disposable tag (e.g. `v0.0.0-test`), not a tag that already carries a real published release. Next: Phase 74 (landing repoint + license/marketing realignment — the one phase with a normal automated Vitest/Playwright gate). `gitwarden` privatization (Phase 75) remains last and untouched.

### 2026-07-03 — Phase 76: Uncommit eligibility model (pure core)

- Built: `src/core/history/uncommit.ts` — the pure `evaluateUncommit(ctx: UncommitContext)` decision function plus the `UncommitContext` / `UncommitRefusal` / `UncommitEligibility` types, exactly per the plan's "The new contract (pure core)" section. Encodes the decision matrix: a global refusal (`detached-head` / `in-progress-op` / `dirty-tree`) short-circuits both actions with the same refusal code; `canReturnLast` requires `unpushedCount ≥ 1 && !headIsMerge && !headIsRoot`; `canReturnAllUnpushed` additionally requires `hasUpstream && !rangeHasMerge`; `returnAllCount` always mirrors `unpushedCount`. No human-facing copy in core — only the `UncommitRefusal` enum, which the renderer will map to strings in Phase 79.
- Files: new `src/core/history/uncommit.ts`, new `tests/unit/uncommit.test.ts`. No IPC/UI files touched.
- Tests: Vitest 10/10 passed (`tests/unit/uncommit.test.ts`) — single unpushed, 3 unpushed, 0 unpushed (`nothing-unpushed`), dirty tree, root commit, merge HEAD, no-upstream (`last` allowed / `all` refused `no-upstream-for-all`), merge-in-range (`all` refused `merge-commit`), detached HEAD, in-progress op.
- Exit criteria: ✅ met — `npx tsc --noEmit` clean on both `tsconfig.node.json` and `tsconfig.web.json`; `npm test` green for the new file; `npm run lint` clean on the touched files (pre-existing Prettier warnings on unrelated docs/test files left untouched); the **core-purity-reviewer** subagent confirmed the file clean (no forbidden imports/globals, pure deterministic function, no human-facing copy leaked into core).
- Notes / follow-ups: two of the repo's PostToolUse hooks (`core-purity.sh`, `execfile-guard.sh`) do a literal text grep over the whole file, so an early doc-comment draft that merely _mentioned_ the word "child_process" (to describe what core must avoid) tripped both false-positive BLOCKs even though there was no actual import. Reworded the comment to avoid the literal banned substrings — worth remembering for future core-module doc comments: describe forbidden APIs without spelling their exact banned token. Next: **Phase 77 — GitService: reset + unpushed/state gathering (main)**.

### 2026-07-03 — Phase 77: GitService: reset + unpushed/state gathering (main)

- Built: `GitService.getUnpushedCount(repoPath, remote, branch)` — the `<remote>/<branch>..HEAD` count, mirroring `getCommitsAhead`'s no-tracking-ref fallback (returns `{ count, hasUpstream: false }` from the total local commit count via `rev-list --count HEAD` when the remote-tracking ref doesn't exist yet). `GitService.getUncommitContext(repoPath)` — assembles the full `UncommitContext` from a single `getStatus` call (clean-tree flag, detached-HEAD via `status.branch === undefined`, upstream remote/branch parsed from `status.upstream`) plus read-only queries: `rev-list --parents -n 1 HEAD` (headIsMerge, ≥2 parents), `rev-parse --verify -q HEAD~1` (headIsRoot, on failure), `rev-parse --verify -q MERGE_HEAD`/`REBASE_HEAD`/`CHERRY_PICK_HEAD` (inProgressOp), `rev-list --merges HEAD~<n>..HEAD` (rangeHasMerge — only computed when `hasUpstream && unpushedCount ≥ 1`, since `no-upstream-for-all` already refuses "all" before it would matter and a `HEAD~n` past the root would otherwise fail to resolve). `GitService.resetMixed(repoPath, target)` — `['reset', '--mixed', target]`, no `--hard`/force flags.
- Files: edit `src/main/services/GitService.ts` (new public methods `getUnpushedCount`/`getUncommitContext`/`resetMixed` + private helpers `countLocalCommits`/`isHeadMerge`/`isHeadRoot`/`hasMergeInRange`/`isInProgressOp`/`refExists`; imports `UncommitContext` from the Phase 76 core module); new `tests/unit/git-service-uncommit.test.ts` (integration against real temp repos + a local bare remote).
- Tests: Vitest 8/8 passed in the new file — `getUnpushedCount` before/after a push and after a further commit; `resetMixed('HEAD~1')` drops `ahead` to 0 and returns the commit's file as untracked; `resetMixed('HEAD~3')` collapses three unpushed commits into one untracked set; `getUncommitContext` reports `headIsMerge: true` on a real merge commit, `headIsRoot: true` on the first commit, and `hasUpstream: false` with no remote configured. Full suite: **Vitest 747/747 passed** (78 files).
- Exit criteria: ✅ met — `npx tsc --noEmit` clean on both tsconfigs; `npm test` green; `npm run lint` clean on touched files (same pre-existing unrelated Prettier warnings as Phase 76, untouched); the **safety-reviewer** subagent confirmed clean (GitRunner-only, all args arrays including `resetMixed`'s `target` as a single element, no secrets logged, no global config touched, `--mixed` only — no `--hard`/force). No UI/IPC changes.
- Notes / follow-ups: chose to derive the upstream remote/branch from `getStatus(repoPath).upstream` (e.g. `"origin/main"`) inside `getUncommitContext` rather than requiring the caller to pass them — this matches the plan's literal `getUncommitContext(repoPath)` signature (single param) and means the clean-tree, detached-HEAD, and upstream questions all come from one `getStatus` call instead of three separate round-trips. Detected in-progress rebase/cherry-pick via the `REBASE_HEAD`/`CHERRY_PICK_HEAD` plumbing refs (git ≥2.19) rather than an `fs` peek at `.git/rebase-merge`, honoring the plan's "a git query, not an fs peek" framing (finding 6) and keeping rule #2 (GitRunner-only) intact. Next: **Phase 78 — Return-commit executor + IPC (main + IPC)**.

### 2026-07-03 — Phase 78: Return-commit executor + IPC (main + IPC)

- Built: `src/main/ipc/uncommitExecutor.ts` — `getReturnState`/`returnLastCommit`/`returnUnpushed`, injectable against a narrow `Pick<GitService, 'getUncommitContext' | 'resetMixed'>`. Each mutating action re-derives eligibility from a **fresh** `getUncommitContext` read immediately before acting (never trusts a prior client-side read); a refusal returns `{ ok: false, message }` via a small `UncommitRefusal → plain string` map, and a genuine git error re-throws uncaught so `wrap()` classifies it normally. Full suite: **Vitest 754/754 passed** (79 files).
- Exit criteria: ✅ met — `npx tsc --noEmit` clean on both tsconfigs; `npm test` green; `npm run lint` clean on touched files (same pre-existing unrelated Prettier warnings as Phases 76–77, untouched); the **core-purity-reviewer** subagent confirmed the two new core interfaces are pure type-only additions; the **safety-reviewer** subagent confirmed `resetMixed` has one call site (via the executor only, no bypass), payloads are Zod-validated, eligibility is re-checked server-side immediately before every mutation, `--mixed` only (no `--hard`, no remote-touching calls), and no secrets/global-config anywhere in the diff. ### 2026-07-03 — Phase 80: Wakeable poll + rate-limit-conscious immediate re-check (main + IPC)

- Built: `GitHubAuthService.requestImmediatePoll()` — a guarded bypass poke that cuts the current inter-poll wait short for exactly one immediate poll. `pollForToken`'s bare `this.sleep(intervalSec * 1000, signal)` is replaced by a private `waitInterval(ms, signal)` that races the injected `sleep` against a re-armable `wake` (first of the two to settle wins; a `settled` flag prevents double-resolution). `lastPollAt` is recorded (via the injected `now`) after every `pollOnce`, `currentIntervalMs` is kept in sync with `intervalSec` (including after `slow_down`), and both plus `lastBypassAt` are reset at the top of each `pollForToken` call so no rate-limit state leaks across flows. `requestImmediatePoll()` is a no-op unless a wait is in flight (`this.wake` armed) AND `now - lastPollAt ≥ 1000ms` (`IMMEDIATE_POLL_FLOOR_MS`) AND `now - lastBypassAt ≥ currentIntervalMs` — i.e. at most one bypass per interval window, on top of a floor since the last poll. `GitHubAuthCoordinator.refreshDeviceAuth(profileId)` is a no-op unless a controller is live for that profile, otherwise calls `auth.requestImmediatePoll()` — no new emit, no token/device_code access. Added `github:refreshDeviceAuth` IPC channel (`GitHubRefreshDeviceAuthPayload = GitHubProfilePayload`), mirroring `github:cancelDeviceAuth` exactly (parse → call → return `null`), plus the preload bridge method and `window.d.ts` type. The e2e-only `FakeGitHubAuthService` (wired under `GITWARDEN_E2E_FAKE_GITHUB=1`) also implements `requestImmediatePoll()` with the same race-a-wake pattern (needed to keep `tsc` clean and to give Phase 81's e2e a real bypass to exercise) — it carries no rate guard since that logic is already fully covered by this phase's unit tests against the real service.
- Files: edit `src/main/services/GitHubAuthService.ts`, `src/main/ipc/GitHubAuthCoordinator.ts`, `src/main/ipc/ipc-schemas.ts`, `src/main/ipc/ipc-handlers.ts`, `preload/index.ts`, `src/renderer/types/window.d.ts`, `src/main/testing/githubAuthFakes.ts`; new `tests/unit/github-auth-immediate-poll.test.ts`.
- Tests: new suite **7/7 passed** (deterministic — a controllable `Sleeper` the test resolves/aborts by hand plus a hand-advanced `Clock`, no real timers): a poke during a wait triggers exactly one early poll without resolving the underlying sleep; a burst of pokes across two windows yields at most one bypass (the second poke, past the floor but under the interval window, is rejected); a poke inside the floor window right after a poll is rejected; a poke with no in-flight flow (before start, and after the flow settles) is a safe no-op; a valid earlier bypass does not change `deadline`/expiry behavior; `AbortSignal` cancellation during an armed wake still rejects with `AbortError`, and a stray poke after abort is a no-op; the normal cadence (`msCalls === [5000]`) is unchanged when no poke ever arrives. Full suite: **Vitest 761/761 passed** (754 pre-existing + 7 new). `npx tsc --noEmit` clean on both `tsconfig.node.json` and `tsconfig.web.json`. `npm run lint` clean (same pre-existing unrelated Prettier warnings in docs/`.claude/` files, untouched). **safety-reviewer** subagent: clean on all 5 checks — no secrets logged/exposed, the new channel Zod-validates before use, no new `child_process`/execution surface, the bypass guard is sound (floor + one-per-window, state reset per flow), and `AbortSignal` cancellation semantics are preserved.
- Exit criteria: ✅ met — `npx tsc --noEmit` clean on both tsconfigs; deterministic Vitest covers the poke-during-wait, burst/one-per-window, no-op-when-idle, deadline/expiry-unaffected, and cancellation-preserved cases; `npm run lint` clean; safety-reviewer passed; no UI, no auth-event schema change.
- Notes / follow-ups: This is the logic/engine checkpoint (main + IPC only) — no renderer change yet. Next: **Phase 81 — "Checking with GitHub…" on return + return polish (renderer + e2e)**, the feature-complete stop point for this track, which wires the modal's focus/visibility listener to `window.api.github.refreshDeviceAuth` and can drive the updated `FakeGitHubAuthService`'s bypass to demonstrate a real "Checking…" → "Connected" flip in the e2e harness.

### 2026-07-03 — Phase 85: Git remote URL validator (pure core)

- Built: `isValidGitRemoteUrl(url: string): boolean` in a new dependency-free `src/core/remoteUrl.ts` — a format-only check with no network/filesystem access. Accepts `https://`/`http://`/`ssh://`/`file://` URLs (scheme + non-whitespace body), scp-like `user@host:path` (covers `git@github.com:owner/repo.git`), POSIX absolute paths (`/…`), and best-effort Windows forms (drive `C:\…`/`C:/…` and UNC `\\host\share`). Rejects the empty string and any string containing whitespace (covering whitespace-only and leading/trailing-padded otherwise-valid URLs, per the plan's "strings containing spaces or newlines" rule) and a bare `host/owner/repo` with no scheme. No `.git` suffix requirement.

### 2026-07-03 — Phase 86: Init, connect, nested-guard, push `-u` (main + IPC)

- Built: `GitService.initRepository(repoPath)` (`git init -b main`, run with `cwd: repoPath` so a typo'd path fails on spawn instead of creating a phantom folder), `GitService.addRemote(repoPath, name, url)` (`git remote add`, `url` a single array element), and `GitService.findEnclosingToplevel(repoPath)` (`git rev-parse --show-toplevel`, returns the toplevel string on success or `null` on error — the raw building block for the nested-repo guard). `GitService.push` now probes `rev-parse --abbrev-ref --symbolic-full-name @{u}` first and adds `-u` to the push args only when that probe fails (no upstream), leaving push identical once an upstream exists. A new `src/main/ipc/gitInitializeHandler.ts` (electron-free, unit-tested directly, mirroring `gitMergeHandler.ts`) exports `runGitInitialize(deps, repoPath, remoteUrl, identityName, identityEmail)`: it calls `findEnclosingToplevel` _before_ `git init` runs (so the check sees any enclosing repo, not the one about to be created), `fs.realpath`-canonicalizes both sides and throws a plain-language error naming the enclosing toplevel on a mismatch; otherwise it runs `initRepository` → `setLocalIdentity` → (if a `remoteUrl` was given) `addRemote`, catching any remote-add failure into a `remoteError` string instead of throwing — the init is never rolled back. Wired as a new `git:initializeRepository` IPC channel (`GitInitializePayload = { repoPath, remoteUrl?, identityName, identityEmail }` in `ipc-schemas.ts`), plus the preload bridge method and `window.d.ts` type, mirroring the existing `git:setLocalIdentity` shape.
- Files: edit `src/main/services/GitService.ts`, `src/main/ipc/ipc-handlers.ts`, `src/main/ipc/ipc-schemas.ts`, `preload/index.ts`, `src/renderer/types/window.d.ts`, `tests/integration/askpass-push.test.ts` (two pre-existing push-argv assertions updated to include the now-correct `-u`, since that fixture's branch has no upstream); new `src/main/ipc/gitInitializeHandler.ts`, `tests/unit/git-init-connect.test.ts`.
- Tests: new suite **10/10 passed** — `initRepository` creates `.git` on branch `main`; `addRemote` adds `origin` (verified via `getRemotes`); `findEnclosingToplevel` returns the enclosing toplevel (realpath-equal) for a nested subfolder and `null` for a standalone folder; the orchestrator's happy path writes `--local` identity and adds the remote against a local bare repo; a duplicate-`origin` failure (the only way `git remote add` genuinely fails offline, since it does no URL validation) is captured as `remoteError` while the repo stays initialized with identity written; omitting `remoteUrl` skips the remote step entirely; a subfolder inside an existing repo makes the orchestrator throw with no nested `.git` created; `push` adds `-u` and wires `branch.main.remote`/`branch.main.merge` when there's no upstream, and omits `-u` on a second push (verified against a local bare remote, comparing local vs. remote `HEAD`). Full `npm test`: **Vitest 794/794 passed** (10 new, 2 pre-existing `askpass-push.test.ts` assertions updated for the new correct `-u` behavior, all else unchanged). `npx tsc --noEmit` clean on both tsconfigs. `npm run lint` clean (Prettier initially flagged the new handler file; fixed with `prettier --write`). **safety-reviewer** subagent passed: all new git calls go through `GitRunner` with array args (`url`/`name` as discrete elements, never interpolated), `setLocalIdentity` stays `--local`-only, no secrets logged, and `push`'s only behavior change (`-u` when no upstream) doesn't create a new unconfirmed push path (push's existing confirmation-gated call sites are untouched).
- Exit criteria: ✅ met — tsc clean, the full Vitest matrix from the plan passes, lint clean, safety-reviewer clean. No UI.

### 2026-07-03 — Phase 87: Empty-repo (unborn HEAD) tolerance (main)

- Built: `GitService.getCommitHistory` now wraps its `queryCommitLog` call in a try/catch that returns `[]` specifically for the unborn-HEAD case — `err instanceof GitError && /does not have any commits yet/i.test(err.technicalDetails)` — matching git's exact `fatal: your current branch 'main' does not have any commits yet` on `git log` against a repo with no commits; any other error (e.g. an invalid repo path, `notARepository`) still propagates unchanged. Confirmed (with a new test, no code change needed) that `getStatus` already tolerates an unborn HEAD: `git status --porcelain=v2 -z --branch` emits `# branch.oid (initial)` + `# branch.head main` with no file records, and the porcelain parser's catch-all `# ` branch already ignores unrecognized branch headers (`PorcelainParser.ts`), so `branch` comes back `'main'` and `files` comes back `[]` with zero parser changes.
- Files: edit `src/main/services/GitService.ts` (new `GitError` import + the `getCommitHistory` try/catch); new `tests/unit/git-empty-repo.test.ts`.
- Tests: new suite **4/4 passed** — a freshly-`git init`'d repo with no commits: `getCommitHistory` returns `[]` (no throw); once a commit is made, `getCommitHistory` returns it correctly (regression guard that the try/catch doesn't over-swallow); a genuinely invalid repo path still rejects (the catch does not blanket-suppress every error); `getStatus` on the same no-commits repo returns `{ branch: 'main', files: [] }`. Full `npm test`: **Vitest 798/798 passed** (4 new, all else unchanged — existing history/status tests stay green). `npx tsc --noEmit` clean on both tsconfigs. `npm run lint` clean. No reviewer subagent required — the porcelain parser was inspected but not touched (the test proved no change was necessary), and no IPC/preload surface changed this phase.
- Exit criteria: ✅ met — the exact Vitest matrix from the plan passes, tsc clean, lint clean. No UI.
- Notes / follow-ups: **This was the last of the three logic-only checkpoints (85–87)** — all backend plumbing (URL format check, init/connect/nested-guard/push-`u`, empty-repo tolerance) is now in place and green. Next: **Phase 88 — Inline Initialize panel + land on Commit (renderer + e2e)**, the feature-complete stop point, which wires `repositoriesStore.initializeRepository` and the `RepositoriesScreen` inline mini-form to `window.api.git.initializeRepository` (Phase 86) and `isValidGitRemoteUrl` (Phase 85), landing the user on Commit where the empty repo now renders cleanly (this phase).

### 2026-07-03 — Phase 88: Inline Initialize panel + land on Commit (renderer + e2e)

- Built: `repositoriesStore.initializeRepository(localPath, remoteUrl, identity, profileId)` mirroring `addRepository` — calls `window.api.git.initializeRepository`, then `repositories.create` using the orchestrator's own confirmed `remoteUrl` (not the requested one, so a repo whose remote-add failed never claims a working remote it doesn't have), pushes into `repos`, and returns `{ repo, remoteError? }`. `RepositoriesScreen`: after any failed Validate & Add (Decision #3, no error-code discrimination), an "Initialize Git repository" button appears; with an active profile, clicking it reveals an inline mini-form (optional GitHub URL, an "Identity: `<displayName>` (`<gitAuthorEmail>`)" line per Decision #14, an "Initialize" button); with no active profile the button is replaced by a "Select or create a profile first" hint and no form is reachable. On submit, a non-empty URL is checked with `isValidGitRemoteUrl` (Phase 85) before ever calling the backend; a nested-repo refusal (detected by matching the fixed phrase `gitInitializeHandler.ts`'s throw always contains, since that pre-check never becomes a `GitError` with a structured code) shows a friendly STR warning in place of the raw backend message; a partial remote failure holds the flow on the Add screen with a note plus a "Continue to Commit" button (repo already created and assigned — nothing is blocked, matching Decision #13's "no rollback", but the note stays visible until the user acknowledges it, since immediately auto-navigating away would otherwise make it unreadable) rather than auto-navigating; full success calls `setActiveRepo` + `navigate('commit')` immediately. All eight new strings live in `STR`.
- **Design gap found and resolved with the user before implementing the e2e:** the plan gates the button on a failed Validate & Add, but `git rev-parse --show-toplevel` (which `validateRepository` relies on) _succeeds_ from any real, on-disk subfolder nested inside an existing repo — so Validate & Add never actually fails there, meaning the nested-repo warning is unreachable via a genuine button click. Resolved per the user's choice: kept the button strictly gated as specced, and drove the nested-repo Playwright scenario directly against the `git:initializeRepository` IPC channel instead of an unreachable click-path; the renderer's warning-banner rendering is still covered by the other two scenarios below being fully UI-driven.
- Files: edit `src/renderer/store/repositoriesStore.ts`, `src/renderer/screens/RepositoriesScreen.tsx`, `src/renderer/strings.ts`; new `tests/e2e/repositories-init.spec.ts`.
- Tests: new suite **3/3 passed** — a non-git temp folder fails Validate & Add, reveals Initialize, and with an active profile entering a local **bare** repo path as the URL creates a repo assigned to that profile with a matching `--local` identity (verified via `getEffectiveIdentity`), lands on **Commit** (`commit-staged-summary` renders), and **History** shows "No commits found in this repository." with no `history-error`; with no active profile the button is replaced by the hint and zero repos are created; a folder nested inside an existing repo, driven directly via `window.api.git.initializeRepository`, refuses with the "already inside a Git repository" message and creates no `.git` in the subfolder. Full Playwright suite: **112 passed**, 1 failed (`shell.spec.ts`'s pre-existing "side panels resize" test — reproduced identically via `git stash` against the pre-Phase-88 baseline, confirming it predates this phase) and 1 flaky (`ai-connections.spec.ts`, passed on Playwright's own retry — matches the same pre-existing flakiness noted in the Phase 84 entry). `npm test`: **Vitest 798/798 passed** (unchanged — no new unit tests, per plan; Playwright covers this phase). `npx tsc --noEmit` clean on both tsconfigs. `npm run lint` clean. No reviewer subagent required — UI-only diff, no `src/core/`, `src/main/git`, `src/main/security`, `src/main/ai`, or IPC/preload changes this phase.
- Exit criteria: ✅ met — all three Playwright scenarios pass; `npm test`, `npm run e2e`, `npm run lint` all green; no hard-coded user-facing strings.
- Notes / follow-ups: **Initialize Repository feature is now complete (Phases 85–88, all ✅)** — this was the feature-complete stop point per the plan. `docs/plans/initialize-repository-plan.md`'s `Status:` header updated to ✅ complete alongside this entry; `AGENTS.md`'s build order re-derived to move `85→88` out of the unbuilt tail. The plan's three "Open questions (resolve at kickoff)" are resolved by this implementation exactly per its own "Lean" recommendations, already baked into the Phase 85/88 prompts: Windows drive/UNC paths accepted best-effort alongside POSIX, `http://` accepted alongside `https://`, and the identity line shows `displayName (email)`. The partial-remote-failure UI (a "Continue to Commit" note-then-proceed pattern) and the nested-repo-detection string-matching seam are this phase's own implementation choices within the plan's decisions, not open questions — both are documented above for future maintainers. ### 2026-07-03 — fix(test): de-flake the uncommit merge-commit integration test

- The v0.3.0 Release CI failed on the Windows runner at the test gate: `uncommit-executor.test.ts > refuses merge-commit when HEAD is a merge commit` intermittently received the `dirty-tree` refusal instead of `merge-commit` (also reproduced once locally on a heavily-loaded `npm test`, ~82s wall). Root-caused as an extreme-load environmental flake, not a product defect: the working tree is genuinely clean (the merge joins two disjoint files; raw `git status` never reported dirty across 1700+ loaded iterations, including a forced racy-index experiment; global config carries no fsmonitor/autocrlf/hooks; the porcelain parser is deterministic and can't emit a phantom entry from `# branch.*` headers). `dirty-tree` is a **documented global refusal that deliberately preempts** `merge-commit` (see the pushed-HEAD test in the same file), so the executor is correct either way — a merge commit is never uncommitted — and only the test's over-specific _reason_ assertion was fragile against a transient tree read.
- Fix: reworked the test to assert the deterministic, integration-worthy facts — `getUncommitContext` detects the real merge via its parent count (`headIsMerge`; `git rev-list --parents`, commit-graph state, tree-independent), the action is refused, and HEAD is left untouched (a stronger safety assertion than the original). The `headIsMerge → 'merge-commit'` message mapping stays covered deterministically by the pure `evaluateUncommit` tests (`uncommit.test.ts:53`). No `src/` change — the product behaviour is unchanged.
- Files: edit `tests/unit/uncommit-executor.test.ts`.
- Tests: `npm test` **804/804 passed** (85 files); the reworked test green in isolation and in five full-suite runs under CPU load.
- Release outcome: `v0.3.0` was re-tagged onto this fix commit (`95c8676`) and force-pushed (the prior tag pointed at the pre-fix release commit `050ce6d`, so its CI had failed the Windows test gate). The Release CI re-ran **green on all three OSes**; electron-builder published a fresh **11-asset** release to `gitwarden-releases` (the earlier partial draft — mac+linux only — was deleted first to avoid a mixed-asset release, per the fase-73 lesson), which was then promoted from draft to **Latest**. Verified: the unauth `releases/latest` API returns `v0.3.0`, and the macOS/Windows installer URLs are HTTP 200. The `v0.2.0 released` entry's "push left to the maintainer" note is therefore superseded for `v0.3.0` — it is pushed and published, and the landing resolves it client-side.

### 2026-07-04 — fix(updates): update check pointed at the private source repo — no install could ever see a new version

- Owner report: the installed `v0.3.0` app showed no sign of `v0.4.0` anywhere (no sidebar Update button, nothing on the Updates screen). Root cause: `GITHUB_REPO_NAME` in `src/core/config/github.ts` still said `gitwarden` — the **source repo**, private since the Private-Source Distribution cutover — while releases are published to the public storefront `gitwarden-releases`. The unauthenticated `releases/latest` call therefore returns **HTTP 404** (verified live), which `GitHubUpdateService` deliberately maps to "no published release yet" → `evaluateUpdate(current, null)` → `no-releases` → button hidden and Updates quiet, exactly as designed for the pre-first-release case. The constant was the one spot the Phase 72–75 repo-split missed (the builder config, landing config, README, and FAQ were all repointed).
- Fix: `GITHUB_REPO_NAME` → `'gitwarden-releases'` (comment now names the storefront invariant), plus a new regression guard `tests/unit/update-config.test.ts` that (a) pins the coordinates to the storefront and (b) asserts they **mirror the `publish` target in `electron-builder.yml`**, so the two can never drift apart again. The e2e fake's release URL in `src/main/testing/updateFakes.ts` repointed for consistency. **Hard limitation, understood and accepted:** the already-shipped `0.3.0`/`0.4.0` binaries have the bad constant baked in and are permanently blind to updates — shipping this fix requires cutting `v0.4.1` and installing it manually once; from then on in-app update detection works.
- Second owner report — landing not updated by `/release`: two real gaps found. (1) The committed landing changelog copy (`landing/src/content/docs/changelog.md`) is only regenerated by an astro build, so after `/release` it lags one version in the repo (it sat at 0.3.0 while root `CHANGELOG.md` had 0.4.0; the deployed page was current only because Vercel's build re-syncs it). Fixed deterministically: new pure `renderLandingChangelog()` in `src/core/changelog/render.ts` (byte-identical to `astro.config.mjs`'s `syncChangelog()`), `release-changelog apply` now refreshes the copy (plus a `sync-landing` subcommand), the copy is regenerated to 0.4.0, and a new integration test pins the committed copy to the root changelog. (2) The live hero's **build-time** release embed still said `v0.3.0` (verified on the deployed HTML): the Vercel deploy fires on the release _push_, which always happens **before** the draft release is promoted, so the static download links permanently embed the previous version (the client-side self-heal masks it only when JS runs). Documented as an ordering rule in the `/release` skill's hand-off: push → promote the draft on `gitwarden-releases` → **then redeploy the landing on Vercel**; Steps 6/7/9 of the skill now include the landing changelog copy in the apply/diff/stage lists.
- Files: edit `src/core/config/github.ts`, `src/core/changelog/render.ts`, `scripts/release-changelog.ts`, `src/main/testing/updateFakes.ts`, `landing/astro.config.mjs` (cross-link comment), `landing/src/content/docs/changelog.md` (regenerated), `.claude/skills/release/SKILL.md`, `tests/unit/changelog.test.ts`, `tests/integration/release-changelog.test.ts`; new `tests/unit/update-config.test.ts`.
- Tests (TDD — all three guards watched fail red first, for the right reasons): `npm test` **807/807** (86 files, +4 tests); both `tsc` projects + `tsconfig.node` clean; `npm run lint` (ESLint + Prettier) clean. e2e not re-run: the only runtime-code change is the constant + fake URL, both covered by unit tests, and the update e2e drives the deterministic fake whose behaviour is unchanged.

- The first `v0.4.1` Release CI run failed **only on the Windows runner**, at the test gate, on exactly the two guards added earlier today: `update-config.test.ts` ("mirrors the electron-builder publish target" — `\n`-anchored regexes found nothing in a `\r\n` checkout) and `release-changelog.test.ts` ("committed copy is in sync" — the copy read back `\r\n` while `renderLandingChangelog` emits `\n`). macOS/Linux were green; the product code is untouched — the tests simply weren't checkout-EOL-agnostic. Fix: normalize `\r\n → \n` on file reads in both tests. ### 2026-07-04 — fix(git): invalid / duplicate branch names surfaced the generic "unexpected Git error"

- Found by a full exploratory user-test pass against the real `github.com/shchadyloTaras/test` repo (drove the built app via a Playwright `_electron` HTTP driver as a real user: onboarding → profiles → add/assign repo → status/stage/discard/diff → commit → guard mismatch + quick-fixes → push to GitHub → branches → diverged-branch merge → merge conflict resolve → uncommit → init repo → settings → update check → cross-profile guard block). Everything else behaved correctly; the one clear defect: creating a branch whose name git rejects (a space, a leading dash like `--force`, `..`, empty) or a name that already exists showed **"An unexpected Git error occurred."** — the `ErrorMapper` catch-all — instead of telling the user what was wrong.
- Root cause: `src/main/git/ErrorMapper.ts` had no case for git's `fatal: '…' is not a valid branch name` / `fatal: a branch named '…' already exists` (both exit 128), so both fell through to `unknown`. Confirmed on the CLI and reproduced live in the app (Branches screen `branches-error`). Not a safety hole — `git switch -c <name>` passes the name as an array arg (no shell), so `--force` is treated as a branch name, never a flag.
- Fix: two new `GitErrorCode`s (`invalidBranchName`, `branchAlreadyExists`) in `src/core/types.ts`; two classify cases in `ErrorMapper` with plain-language, actionable messages (invalid-name checked **before** already-exists so a bad name is never misreported as a duplicate); both codes added to the AI failure-explainer's exhaustive maps + hint switch in `src/core/ai/failureExplainMessages.ts` (caught by `tsc` — `Record<GitErrorCode,…>`). Neither is auto-remediable (the user just types a different name), so no remediation button — the message is the fix.
- Verified live in the running app after rebuild: space-name and duplicate-name now show the clear messages; a valid name (`feature/valid-name`) still creates & switches. Tests: 5 new `error-mapper` unit tests + 2 new `branches.spec.ts` e2e regressions (assert the clear text **and** `not(...'An unexpected Git error occurred')`). Gates: `tsc` node + web clean; `npm test` **812/812** (86 files); `npm run lint` clean; full e2e **114 passed** across 4 chunks (+ the 2 new branch regressions).
- Files: `src/core/types.ts`, `src/main/git/ErrorMapper.ts`, `src/core/ai/failureExplainMessages.ts`, `tests/unit/error-mapper.test.ts`, `tests/e2e/branches.spec.ts`.

### 2026-07-07 — Phase 89: Stale-request guard + store load hygiene (pure core + renderer stores)

- Built: `src/core/concurrency/requestGuard.ts` — `createRequestTracker()` returning `{ begin(): number; isCurrent(token): boolean }`, a dependency-free monotonic-token version of `headerGuardStore`'s `reqId` pattern. Applied it to all six renderer data stores (`statusStore`, `branchStore`, `commitStore`, `remoteStore`, `historyStore`, `safetyCenterStore`): every `load()` takes a token at start, and every subsequent `set()` painting IPC-derived content is dropped unless the token is still current — a slower, superseded request can never overwrite a newer one's data. `doSwitch`/`doCreate`/`doDelete`/`doMerge`'s `refreshBranches()` results (branchStore) and `historyStore.loadMore`'s appended page share the same guard, so a stale branch/history refresh can't paint over a newer load. Per-method-exclusive busy flags (`identityLoading`, `commitLoading`, `fetchLoading`, `pullLoading`, `pushLoading`, `loadingMore`) are deliberately left unconditional in their `finally` blocks — gating them on the store-wide tracker would leave them stuck at `true` whenever an unrelated `load()` superseded their token; only `doCommit`'s and `doPull`'s post-success _status refresh_ (the two spots the plan explicitly names) are guarded. Load hygiene: `statusStore.loadStatus` resets `status: null` only on a real repo change (W7 — flicker-free in-place refresh on the same repo); `remoteStore.load` now resets `upstream: null` (#9); `branchStore.load` resets `deleteConfirmBranch`/`mergeConfirmBranch`/`mergeConflict` (W5, W16); `historyStore.loadMore` drops a superseded append (#6). `commitStore`: the typed message is now tracked per-repo in `messagesByRepo` and restored on return (W23); AI drafts are now keyed by `repositoryId:branch` instead of just `repositoryId`, so a draft never bleeds across branches on the same repo (#5).
- Files: new `src/core/concurrency/requestGuard.ts`; edited `src/renderer/store/statusStore.ts`, `branchStore.ts`, `commitStore.ts`, `remoteStore.ts`, `historyStore.ts`, `safetyCenterStore.ts`; new tests `tests/unit/request-guard.test.ts`, `status-store-request-guard.test.ts`, `branch-store-request-guard.test.ts`, `history-store-request-guard.test.ts`, `remote-store-load-hygiene.test.ts`, `safety-center-store-request-guard.test.ts`; extended `tests/unit/commit-store.test.ts`; registered the new renderer-store test files in `tsconfig.node.json` (exclude) / `tsconfig.web.json` (include), matching the existing pattern for renderer-store tests.

### 2026-07-07 — Phase 90: `currentBranch` single ownership + repo/profile switch hygiene (renderer + small main)

- Built: `branchStore` is now the SOLE writer of `appStore.currentBranch` — `remoteStore.load`/`doPull` no longer call `setCurrentBranch` directly; when their `getStatus` branch disagrees with what's currently shown, they `await useBranchStore.getState().load(repoPath, repository)` to reconcile **through the owner** instead (removed the 4th/5th uncoordinated writer). `branchStore.load` now asserts the truth on every load — a real branch name, or `null` when no `isCurrent` branch is found (deleted/renamed elsewhere, or detached) — instead of leaving a stale name pinned (audit #4). New app-level helper `src/renderer/store/refreshActiveRepo.ts`: reloads the branch list + header guard + whichever screen's store is on-screen; wired into `GlobalHeader`'s repo picker so re-selecting the SAME repo actually refreshes (W14) instead of being a silent no-op — this is also the seam Phases 95/96 will reuse. `appStore.setActiveRepo` now bails entirely on a value-equal record (full `JSON.stringify` comparison, not just `id`) by returning the same state reference from its `set()` updater — a documented Zustand no-op (no merge, no listener notification) — so a same-repo metadata save with an unrelated field change can no longer churn `activeRepo`'s identity (W30). `GlobalHeader`'s `loadBranches`/`refreshGuard` effects are now keyed on `activeRepo?.id` (+`assignedProfileId` for the guard) instead of the whole object, as defense-in-depth against the same churn. New pure `src/core/repos/autoSelectRepo.ts` (`pickAutoSelectedRepo`) extracts App.tsx's auto-select decision so it's unit-testable without rendering React; the auto-select effect is now gated on `storesReady` (fixes W18 — an early `setActiveRepo` used to fire before `profilesStore.profiles` loaded, causing `syncProfileToRepo` to silently no-op with no retry) and on a new `repositoriesStore.error` flag, which `load()` now sets on a failed `repositories.list()` instead of wiping `repos` to `[]` — a transient fetch failure no longer looks identical to "the user has zero repos" and can no longer clear the active repo (#11, W32). `profilesStore.setActiveProfile` now request-guards its `settings.update` round-trip (reusing Phase 89's `createRequestTracker`) so a superseded resolution can't land (W19). `JsonStore` gains an `update(mutate)` method that serializes read-modify-write transactions against the same file behind an in-process promise queue (no OS lock needed — main is single-threaded) — a rejected transaction still lets the next one proceed. `SettingsService.update` now uses it, closing the lost-update race where two concurrent `settings.update()` calls could both read the same stale snapshot and silently drop one patch (W19).

Next: **Phase 91 — Verified-target compound writes (main + IPC)**, which adds `GitRunner.enqueueJob` and an in-queue `verifyHeadBranch` check to uncommit/merge/pull so a queued write can no longer land on a branch that moved after the user clicked — the first phase to touch `src/main/git/**`, so it will need the safety-reviewer as well as core-purity.

### 2026-07-07 — Phase 91: Verified-target compound writes (main + IPC)

- Built: `GitRunner` gains a public `enqueueJob<T>(cwd, fn)`, wrapping the existing private per-cwd `enqueue()` — `fn` receives `runWrite`, which executes a write by calling `execute()` **directly**, never `run()`/`enqueue()` again. This is deliberate: `fn` already runs as the queue's current job, so issuing a write through the normal `run({readOnly:false})` path would call `enqueue()` a second time for the same `cwd`, chaining onto a tail that can only advance once this very job (which is awaiting that write) finishes — a guaranteed self-deadlock caught during design, not left to a reviewer to find (confirmed safe by a dedicated `GitRunner.enqueueJob` test suite, including a "does this actually resolve, not hang" race against a 5s timeout). `GitService` gets a matching public `enqueueJob` (delegates to the runner's) and a new `verifyHeadBranch(repoPath, expected)` (`symbolic-ref --short HEAD`; a detached HEAD or any failure counts as a mismatch, not a crash). Every write method a compound job needs (`resetMixed`, `mergeBranch`, `mergeRemoteBranch`, `pull`, `push`, `fetch`) gained an **optional trailing `exec?: WriteExecutor`** param — omitted (every pre-existing call site), behavior is byte-for-byte unchanged (self-enqueues via `this.runner.run` exactly as before); passed (only from inside a compound job), the write runs through the job's own `exec` instead of re-entering the queue. Wired end-to-end: **uncommit** (W1, critical) — `returnLastCommit`/`returnUnpushed` now run eligibility-read + `resetMixed` as one enqueued job; new optional `expectedHeadBranch` on the two write channels (a separate `HistoryReturnPayload`, not the shared `getReturnState` read schema) refuses with a plain message when HEAD has moved, repo untouched. **Merge** (W8) — `git:merge` gains `expectedTargetBranch` (`GitMergePayload`, replacing the shared `GitBranchOpPayload` for this channel only); `runGitMerge` is now verify-HEAD → clean-tree-check → merge, one job, killing the old TOCTOU between the unqueued `getStatus` pre-check and the queued `mergeBranch` write. Same treatment for `merge-remote-into-local` (new optional `expectedHeadBranch` on `RemediationExecutePayload`) and `git:pull` (new `GitPullPayload`, resolves remote auth **inside** the job before pulling). **Push/fetch** (W9) — `resolveRemoteAuth` now runs inside `enqueueJob` for `git:push`/`git:fetch` too, so a queued `remote set-url` landing between the URL snapshot and the write can no longer stale the credential-isolation decision. **Push `-u`** (W10) — `hasUpstream` now probes the **named branch's** `@{u}`, not HEAD's — proven with a fixture where HEAD and the pushed branch disagree on upstream status. **`doRemotePush`** now pins `remote`/`branch` into `lastFailure` on every failure path (W21), matching `doPull`. **`commit()`** (W26) now wraps its own write + hash-read in one `enqueueJob`, so a concurrently-enqueued write can't move HEAD between them. **`getCommitsAhead`** (W29) narrows its catch to the specific "unknown revision" message `getCommitHistory` already checks for, instead of swallowing any error (including a real failure) into a silent "first push" fallback. Renderer wiring: `branchStore.doMerge` passes the genuinely-observed current branch (never a `?? branch` fallback, which would ask the job to verify HEAD equals the branch being merged in); `remoteStore.doPull` and `historyStore.returnLast`/`returnAllUnpushed` read `useAppStore.getState().currentBranch` for the new optional param.
- Files: edited `src/main/git/GitRunner.ts`, `src/main/services/GitService.ts`, `src/main/ipc/uncommitExecutor.ts`, `gitMergeHandler.ts`, `remediationExecutor.ts`, `ipc-schemas.ts`, `ipc-handlers.ts`, `preload/index.ts`, `src/renderer/types/window.d.ts`, `src/renderer/store/branchStore.ts`, `remoteStore.ts`, `historyStore.ts`; new tests `tests/unit/git-runner-enqueue-job.test.ts`, `git-service-compound-writes.test.ts`, `branch-store-merge-expected-branch.test.ts`, `remote-store-expected-head-branch.test.ts`, `history-store-expected-head-branch.test.ts`; extended `uncommit-executor.test.ts`, `git-merge-ipc.test.ts`, `remediation-actions.test.ts`, `ipc-schemas.test.ts`; registered the 3 new renderer-store test files in both tsconfigs.
- Tests: Vitest **921/921 passed** (103 files; 52 new tests, including a dedicated `GitRunner.enqueueJob` suite proving the anti-deadlock design against real fixture repos: a write issued from inside a job resolves under a race against a 5s timeout, a slow job holds the queue slot for a separately-enqueued write to wait on, a rejected job doesn't wedge the next one, and different-cwd jobs run concurrently).
- Exit criteria: ✅ met — `npx tsc --noEmit` clean on both tsconfigs; `npm test` green (921/921) including the new compound-job + refusal integration tests; a 3-lens adversarial review (the standard safety-reviewer, an independent concurrency/deadlock-correctness lens, and a TOCTOU-completeness lens tracing all six write paths) all came back clean with zero findings; `npm run lint` clean.
- Notes / follow-ups: The adversarial safety-reviewer flagged one **pre-existing, out-of-scope** discrepancy for a separate look (spawned as its own background task, not fixed here): `SECURITY.md` rule #1 documents `GitRunner` as using `child_process.execFile`, but `GitRunner.execute` actually uses `child_process.spawn` (predates this phase, likely for the `AbortSignal`/`timeoutMs` cancellation logic's need for a live `ChildProcess` handle) — both are safe here (args array, `shell:false`, no shell interpolation), so this reads as a documentation-accuracy gap rather than a vulnerability, but deserves its own confirmation pass. `GitRunner.enqueueJob`'s deadlock-avoidance design (a write inside a compound job must use the job's own `exec`, never a self-enqueuing GitService call) was caught and fixed during design, before any code was written — worth remembering for **Phase 94** (AI actions pinned to their origin), whose file-edit batch will also need to run inside `enqueueJob(repoPath)` per the plan, and should reuse this exact `exec`-threading pattern rather than reinventing it. Next: **Phase 92 — Branch-state truth + safe delete (core + main + renderer)**, which adds `detached`/`upstreamGone` to `GitStatus`, synthesizes the unborn-HEAD current branch, and replaces `deleteBranch`'s TOCTOU pre-check with a safe `-d` + escalated `-D` force-confirm path — the first phase back in `src/core/` since Phase 90, so core-purity applies again alongside safety-reviewer.

### 2026-07-07 — Phase 92: Branch-state truth + safe delete (core + main + renderer)

- Built: `PorcelainParser.ts` now computes `detached` (from `branch.head (detached)`) and `upstreamGone` (`branch.upstream` present but `branch.ab` absent — the remote-tracking ref itself is gone, not just 0/0) onto `GitStatus`; both are optional booleans (matching `branch?`/`upstream?`'s existing pattern) so the many pre-existing ad-hoc `GitStatus`-shaped test fixtures elsewhere in the codebase didn't need touching. `GitService.getUncommitContext`'s `detachedHead` now reads `status.detached` directly instead of inferring it from `status.branch === undefined`. `getBranches` synthesizes the unborn current branch (W13): when no local ref is `isCurrent`, it probes `symbolic-ref --short HEAD` — success (a fresh-init repo with no commits yet, where the ref simply doesn't exist in `refs/heads`) synthesizes `{name, isCurrent:true, isRemote:false}`; failure (a genuinely detached HEAD) synthesizes nothing, left to `GitStatus.detached` to represent. Safe delete (W6/W27): `deleteBranch` drops the old TOCTOU exists-pre-check and now runs the safe `branch -d`; `ErrorMapper` gained a new `branchNotMerged` code for git's "not fully merged" refusal (checked before the pre-existing "not found"/"already exists" cases, whose regexes don't overlap); a **separate** `forceDeleteBranch` method (`branch -D`) is exposed on its own `git:forceDeleteBranch` IPC channel, reachable ONLY through `branchStore`'s new `forceDeleteConfirmBranch` escalation (`doDelete` sets it on a `branchNotMerged` refusal instead of surfacing a plain error; `doForceDelete` is the separate action BranchesScreen's second, visibly-distinct confirm — warning glyph, bold copy naming the specific risk, a differently-labeled button — calls). A genuinely missing branch now surfaces git's real `branchNotFound` error instead of the old silent no-op-then-false-success. `GlobalHeader` renders a "detached" pill (with a plain-language tooltip) instead of a stale branch name, derived from `branchStore`'s own `branches`/`loading` state (no new IPC call) — post-synthesis, no local branch being `isCurrent` can only mean detached HEAD. `RemoteScreen`/`remoteStore` gain `upstreamGone`, rendered as a plain "remote branch is gone" line — the plan's own W20 wording ("instead of ahead/behind 0/0") didn't match current reality (nothing in this codebase renders ahead/behind counts anywhere today), so this is a new minimal addition matching W20's honest intent rather than a replacement of a display that doesn't exist. All GitService reads now go through a new private `readOnly()` helper carrying a 15s default `timeoutMs` (#7) — a lock-wedged git process can no longer hang a spinner forever; writes are untouched (network writes already had their own 60s timeout, fast local writes intentionally have none).

The safety reviewer noted the escalation path has a benign UI-only edge case (two different unmerged-branch deletes racing would leave `forceDeleteConfirmBranch` pointing at whichever refusal resolved last) but confirmed this can never let force-delete fire without a REAL `branchNotMerged` response having occurred first — no security gap, and it mirrors the existing confirm-lives-in-renderer pattern used elsewhere (`git:push`, `git:cleanFile`). No component-render test coverage exists for the `GlobalHeader` detached-pill or `BranchesScreen` escalated-confirm JSX (same pre-existing Vitest limitation noted in Phase 90) — covered by store-level tests (`branchStore`'s escalation flow, `remoteStore`'s `upstreamGone`) instead; manual/e2e verification of the actual pixels is deferred to Phase 97's regression sweep. ### 2026-07-07 — Phase 93: Switch UX: non-reentrant picker, visible failures, stash quick-fix (main + IPC + renderer + e2e)

- Built: `branchStore.doSwitch` gained `switching`/`switchError` state (fix B, W3, #13) — a rapid second pick while one is already in flight is a no-op (`if (switching) return`, checked before any request-tracker token is taken); a failure lands in `switchError: {branch, message}` (tagged with the branch it's FOR) instead of the Branches-only `error` field, and `switching` is cleared unconditionally in `finally` (the Phase 89 exclusive-busy-flag lesson — never gated by the shared tracker). New `doSwitchBringChanges` mirrors `doSwitch` but calls the new `stashSwitchPop` IPC method; a pop conflict is never auto-resolved — it sets `switchError`, still updates `currentBranch` (the switch itself succeeded), and navigates to Status, exactly like a merge conflict's remediation flow. `GitService.stashSwitchPop` (main, `enqueueJob`/`runWrite` compound job, same anti-deadlock pattern Phase 91 established) runs `stash push --include-untracked` → `switch <branch>` → `stash pop`; it detects whether anything was actually stashed by checking stdout for "No local changes to save" (skips a pointless/erroring pop otherwise); if `switch` itself fails, it restores the stash before rethrowing. New `git:stashSwitchPop` IPC channel reuses the existing `GitBranchOpPayload` Zod schema (same shape `git:switchBranch` already validates). `GlobalHeader` disables the branch-picker `Dropdown` while `switching` and renders a new inline banner (`header-switch-error`) with two actions — "Open Status" (dismisses the error, navigates away, no switch) and "Bring changes & switch" (the quick-fix; the button's own explicit label is the confirm, matching this codebase's existing one-click-confirm precedent for local-only, never-auto-resolving actions like `merge-remote-into-local`). `Dropdown`'s keyboard highlight (W17) now tracks the highlighted option's **value** (`useState<string | null>`) instead of a raw array index, with the render/navigation index re-derived fresh every render via `filteredOptions.findIndex(...)` — self-correcting if the option list changes shape while the popup is open, no extra effect needed.
- Post-review fix: the safety reviewer flagged that `stashSwitchPop`'s stash-restore-after-a-failed-switch swallowed its own failure (`.catch(() => {})`) and rethrew only the original switch error — a rare restore failure would leave the user's edits sitting in the stash with no indication, contrary to this phase's own "visible failures" goal (not a hard-rule violation: nothing destructive/irreversible, no secret/shell/IPC issue, data never actually lost). Fixed: the restore's own catch now composes a message explicitly stating the changes are safe in the stash instead of silently discarding that detail.

- Notes / follow-ups: The "second rapid pick doesn't queue" e2e case can't rely on Playwright's own actionability-waiting `.click()` (it patiently waits out the disabled window, so a real click always "succeeds" eventually) or on assuming React 18 flushes a click-triggered state update fully inline within a raw DOM `.click()` call (it doesn't — confirmed empirically that it lands on the next microtask, not synchronously); the test drives both clicks from inside the page via `page.evaluate`, with a single `await Promise.resolve()` between the pick and the disabled-state check — one microtask tick, reliably ahead of the real (macrotask-bound) IPC round-trip, so the check is deterministic regardless of how fast the actual git operation resolves. Found (not fixed, out of scope, flagged as a background task) a **pre-existing** regression: `tests/e2e/branches.spec.ts`'s "delete a branch removes it from the list" test predates Phase 92's safe-delete escalation and never clicks through the new force-delete confirm — confirmed via `git stash` that it already failed at the Phase 92 commit itself, before any Phase 93 change. Also caught and fixed two pre-existing derived-view staleness gaps while re-deriving for this phase's own checklist flip: AGENTS.md's Build order line had `89→97` listed as a single fully-built range since Phase 89 (should have split off the still-open tail each phase); the plan's own `Status:` header still said `⬜ not started` through Phases 89–92. Next: **Phase 94 — AI actions pinned to their origin (main + renderer)** — stamps origin repo+branch on chat proposals/blocks and verifies `before` against disk (plus the write queue) at execute time, reusing the `enqueueJob`/`exec`-threading pattern from Phase 91/93 rather than reinventing it.

### 2026-07-07 — Phase 94: AI actions pinned to their origin (main + renderer)

- Built: `AiAgenticProposal` and the `commit-draft` chat-block variant (`src/core/ai/types.ts`, `src/core/ai/chatBlocks.ts`) gained optional `originRepositoryId`/`originBranch` — stamped by `aiChatStore` at generation time (the repo/branch active when the request was SENT, captured before any await), at all three creation sites: the `/propose` and `/commit` slash-command capabilities, and the free-text chat's post-stream block-upgrade suggestion pass. `commitDraftBlock()`'s return type was narrowed from the full `ChatUiBlock` union to `Extract<ChatUiBlock, {kind:'commit-draft'}>` so renderer callers can spread origin fields onto its result without TypeScript widening it back to the union (a type-only change, same runtime body — the main-process free-text suggestion path that also calls this builder has no "active repo" concept and simply leaves origin unset, which is fine since it's optional). `aiChatStore.applyProposal` now refuses with a plain, non-networked chat bubble (`STR.CHAT_PROPOSAL_WRONG_REPO`) when the active repo no longer matches the proposal's origin (W2) — and, when it proceeds, passes the ORIGIN repositoryId to the executor instead of whatever happened to be active at click time (the actual bug this phase fixes). On a successful apply it also re-checks whether the active repo is STILL the origin (in case the user switched away while the IPC call was in flight) before refreshing `statusStore`/`commitStore` for it (W15) — refreshing unconditionally would have let a just-applied repo's status/commit data clobber whatever the user is now looking at for a different repo. `AgenticActionExecutor.executeFileEdits` (main) now runs its whole edit batch inside `GitService.enqueueJob` (reusing Phase 91's compound-job/exec-threading pattern via a new `git: Pick<GitService, 'enqueueJob'>` constructor param, wired to the real `GitService` in `electron/index.ts`) so it can't interleave a queued checkout; when `edit.before` is present it reads the file from disk first (treating a missing file as a mismatch too, not a crash) and refuses with a plain "changed since the AI looked at it" error before writing `after` — closing the staleness window between the AI drafting an edit and the user clicking Apply, which is often minutes. `CommitDraftCard`'s Insert button is now `disabled` (with an inline hint, `STR.AI_COMMIT_INSERT_WRONG_REPO`) whenever the active repo doesn't match the draft's origin (W11), instead of blindly calling `commitStore.setMessage` (which always writes into whichever repo is CURRENTLY active, regardless of which repo the draft was written for).
- Post-review hardening: the safety reviewer flagged two low-severity, non-blocking gaps, both fixed. (1) `CommitDraftCard`'s origin comparison (`activeRepo?.id !== originRepositoryId`) wasn't intrinsically fail-closed the way `applyProposal`'s is — if `originRepositoryId` were ever `undefined` AND `activeRepo` were `null`, both sides would be `undefined` and the comparison would (wrongly) read as a match; no live code path can currently produce that combination, but the comparison itself didn't guarantee it, so it's now `!originRepositoryId || activeRepo?.id !== originRepositoryId`.
