### 2026-07-07 — Phase 95: Focus revalidation + refresh wiring (renderer)

- Built: `App.tsx` gained one new effect adding `window` `focus` + `document` `visibilitychange` listeners (mirroring `ConnectGitHubModal.tsx`'s existing return-focus poke — same boolean+`setTimeout` debounce shape, ≥2s between triggers) that call Phase 90's `refreshActiveRepo()` on every focus/visibility-return — the always-mounted header (branch list, guard) and whichever screen is on-screen finally heal without needing a repo/tab switch (W4-cheap). `refreshActiveRepo()` makes no network call, so it runs unthrottled by `navigator.webdriver` — the new e2e smoke needs it to fire under Playwright too. The SAME trigger also calls `updatesStore`'s new `checkIfStale(now)` (W28), gated behind the SAME `navigator.webdriver` guard the existing launch-time check already uses, so e2e still makes zero real GitHub calls. `checkIfStale` is throttled to ≥24h via a new pure `isStale(lastAt, now, thresholdMs)` helper (`src/core/concurrency/staleness.ts`, sibling to `requestGuard.ts`) — deliberately a TIMESTAMP comparison (not a live boolean+timer like the 2s debounce above), since a timer can't reliably survive a 24h span across app sleep/wake; `now`/`lastAt` are passed in rather than read internally, keeping the throttle decision itself testable without faking timers. `check()` itself (the launch check and the manual "Check for updates" button in Settings) is NEVER throttled — only the focus-triggered `checkIfStale` path is. `safetyCenterStore.load`'s success path now also AWAITS `headerGuardStore.refresh` for the same repo/profile (W12) — awaited (not fire-and-forget) so `refreshActiveRepo()`'s own `Promise.all` sees the guard as already settled when IT resolves; this is on top of, not instead of, GlobalHeader's own independent refresh effect, and closes the one path where the screen and the always-mounted badge could disagree (the screen reloading on its own, e.g. via focus revalidation, without the header's effect also having re-fired for the same reason). `remoteStore.doFetch`'s success path now reloads `remoteStore` itself plus nudges `branchStore`/`historyStore` for the same repo (W25) — previously a fetch's only observable effect was a toast, even though a fetch can move remote-tracking refs (ahead/behind, upstream status) and surface new incoming commits; the reload happens BEFORE the success message is set (since `load()`'s own reset would otherwise wipe it out), and is guarded on the repo not having changed while the (potentially slow, networked) fetch was in flight.

Fixed by having `checkIfStale` overwrite `lastCheckedAt` with its OWN injected `now` after `check()` returns — same real-world behavior, deterministic in tests. The e2e smoke drives the focus/visibilitychange listener via a synthetic `window.dispatchEvent(new Event('focus'))` rather than a real OS-level focus change, matching Phase 93's precedent of driving DOM state directly when real timing/OS behavior would make a test non-deterministic. Next: **Phase 96 — `.git` watcher: instant external-change detection (main + IPC + renderer)** — a `RepoWatcherService` (`fs.watch` on `.git/HEAD`/`.git/refs`/`.git/index`, debounced ~400ms) pushing `repo:changed` events the renderer routes into `refreshActiveRepo(scope)`, replacing the "only heals on focus/switch" ceiling this phase's cheap layer still has.

### 2026-07-07 — Phase 96: `.git` watcher: instant external-change detection (main + IPC + renderer)

- Built: new `src/main/services/RepoWatcherService.ts` — `fs.watch` on the active repo's `.git/HEAD` (single file), `.git/refs` (recursive, so nested `refs/heads/feature/foo`-style branch names are covered — recursive `fs.watch` isn't supported on Linux, so `refs/` falls back to periodic stat-polling there instead of a non-recursive watch that would silently miss nested directories), and `.git/index` (single file, tolerant of not existing yet in a brand-new repo). Debounced ~400ms, collecting distinct kinds seen during the window into a `Set` so a burst of churn (e.g. `git commit -a` touching both index and refs) collapses into at most one event per kind, not one per filesystem notification. Watches only ONE repo at a time — `watch()` always closes any previous watch first, so the renderer never has to reason about multiple live watchers. Events push over a new `repo:changed` IPC channel via `webContents.send` (validated with a new `RepoChangedEventPayload` Zod schema before every send — same pattern as the existing `github:authEvent` channel's `emit()`), exposed through the existing preload event-bridge pattern (`onChanged`, mirroring `onAuthEvent`). New `repo:watch`/`repo:unwatch` IPC handlers, driven by a new `App.tsx` effect keyed on `activeRepo?.localPath` (watch on every active-repo change — `watch()`'s own self-closing makes the effect's cleanup mostly redundant except for the "no active repo at all" case, where nothing else would call `unwatch()`). A second, subscribe-once `App.tsx` effect routes every `repo:changed` event into Phase 90's `refreshActiveRepo(scope)`, which gained a new optional `scope: 'full' | 'index'` parameter (default `'full'`, preserving every existing caller's behavior unchanged): `head`/`refs` map to `'full'` (HEAD/refs could have moved, so branch list + guard + whatever screen is active all need reloading); `index` maps to `'index'` (only Status/Commit, if one of those is the active screen — reloading branch list/guard for a plain stage/unstage would be wasted work).
- Post-review hardening: the safety reviewer flagged that nothing tied the watch's lifetime to the window/webContents holding `sender` — the only cleanup path was the renderer's own React effect cleanup calling `repo:unwatch`, an IPC round-trip that depends on the renderer still being alive to make it, which is exactly the case that's least reliable during an actual window close (this app is single-window, no multi-window support per this track's own Non-goals, so a closed window always means "nothing is watching anymore"). Not a rule violation (bounded exposure — the stray watch/poll interval gets superseded the next time anything calls `repo:watch` again, and ends when the app quits) but a real background-resource-lingering gap. Fixed: `createWindow` now takes the `RepoWatcherService` instance and hooks `win.on('closed', () => repoWatcher.unwatch())` as a belt-and-suspenders main-process-side cleanup, on top of (not instead of) the renderer's own effect cleanup.
- Files: new `src/main/services/RepoWatcherService.ts`; edited `src/main/ipc/ipc-schemas.ts` (`RepoWatchPayload`, `RepoChangedEventPayload`), `src/main/ipc/ipc-handlers.ts` (`repoWatcher` on `Services`, the two new handlers), `electron/index.ts` (construction + wiring + the `closed`-hook fix), `preload/index.ts` (`repo` namespace + `RepoChangedEvent`/`REPO_CHANGED_CHANNEL`), `src/renderer/types/window.d.ts` (mirrors), `src/renderer/store/refreshActiveRepo.ts` (`RefreshScope` param), `src/renderer/App.tsx` (watch/unwatch effect + subscribe-once effect), `AGENTS.md`/`docs/progress-log.md`/the plan's `Status:` header (derived-view re-derive); new `tests/unit/repo-watcher-service.test.ts` (6 tests, real-git integration style — head/refs/index classification, debounce-collapses-a-burst, unwatch-stops-events, watching-a-new-repo-closes-the-old-one).
- Tests: Vitest **991/991 passed** (113 files; 6 new tests, run 3× to rule out flakiness — the head/refs/index fs.watch behavior is inherently timing-sensitive). No new Playwright spec (the plan's own exit criteria for this phase list only tsc/test/lint plus "no watcher leaks (unwatch verified in test)" — no e2e requirement); re-ran `branches.spec.ts`/`focus-refresh.spec.ts` as a sanity check that the new real watch/unwatch IPC calls in the always-mounted `App.tsx` don't regress anything (9/10 passed — the one failure is the already-flagged, unrelated Phase-92 `branches.spec.ts` delete-test gap).
- Exit criteria: ✅ met — `npx tsc --noEmit` clean on both tsconfigs; `npm test` green (991/991) including the watcher integration test; `npm run lint` clean; no watcher leaks (unwatch, and watching-a-different-repo-closes-the-old-watch, both directly verified in the test); safety review clean (one non-rule hardening suggestion, applied above — no `src/core/` files touched this phase, so core-purity-reviewer's trigger rule didn't apply).
- Notes / follow-ups: One test-authoring gotcha worth remembering — the "watching a different repo closes the previous watch" test initially asserted that the ONLY event observed for the newly-watched repo would have `kind: 'index'` (from a deliberate `git add`), but macOS's FSEvents backend can replay a change from just before a watch subscribes (a `refs` event leftover from that repo's own setup commit, which happened moments before `watch()` was called) — relaxed the assertion to check that the expected `index` event arrives, not that it's the only one, matching the plan's own "self-triggered/leftover churn is tolerated" philosophy rather than fighting a benign OS-level characteristic. Next: **Phase 97 — Polish + regression sweep (renderer + e2e)** — the feature-complete stop point for this track: worktree-prune affordance (W22), connect-return reconcile (W24), create-branch-failure keeps the typed name (W31), a full re-verification of `docs/investigations/branch-switch-freshness-audit.md`'s finding list with a per-finding fixed/deferred map, and the track's full regression e2e sweep (chunked — it exceeds 10 min as one block). Per the run-track skill's own rule, **stop the loop and report after this phase's commit**, even though it is itself the last phase in the plan's own numbering.

new `GitService.pruneWorktrees`/`git:pruneWorktrees` IPC channel/`branchStore.doPruneWorktrees` run `git worktree prune`; `BranchesScreen` swaps the plain "In worktree" badge for a "Clean up stale worktree" button (the button's own label is the confirm, AGENTS.md #6 — nothing the user still has is at risk, the directory is already gone) whenever `worktreeMissing` is true, replacing what was previously a permanent Switch/Delete lock with no way out. **Connect-return reconcile (W24)** — `ProfilesScreen`'s `ConnectGitHubModal` `onClose` now always calls `profilesStore.load()`, regardless of why it closed (cancel, success, error) — a cancel racing the OAuth return-poke can no longer leave the UI showing "unlinked" when main already persisted the link. **Create-branch failure keeps the typed name (W31)** — `branchStore.doCreate` now returns `Promise<boolean>` (success/failure) instead of `void`; - **AI commit-draft Insert wiped the drafted message** — FIXED. `commitStore.setMessage` now persists the per-repo message under the active repo (`useAppStore.getState().activeRepo?.id`) when `commitStore.repository` is still null, so the first `load()` on Commit-screen mount restores it instead of overwriting it with the empty saved value. **Attribution correction to the Phase 97 entry above:** it was recorded there as a "pre-existing, AI-Chat-Redesign-track bug," but it is a **Phase 89 regression of _this_ track** — `git blame`/pickaxe put both the overwrite line (`message: … : savedMessage`) and the `messagesByRepo` guard in `a0153b8` (Phase 89). The earlier "git stash against the Phase-96 commit" check reproduced it only because Phase 96 is _after_ Phase 89; a stash to before Phase 89 (Phase 88, `88cf0a6`) does not reproduce it. Regression guard: new unit test in `tests/unit/commit-store.test.ts` (RED→GREEN verified).
- **`diff.spec.ts` "shows diff for an unstaged change" never rendered the panel** — FIXED (test-only). Root cause was the test, not the app: a bare row-centre `.click()` on the narrow file list landed on the right-aligned "Stage" button (which `stopPropagation`s) instead of selecting the row, so the file got staged and no diff showed. Now clicks the filename text, which bubbles to `onSelect`. Also isolated the spec's Electron `userData` via `--user-data-dir` (Electron honors it — probe-confirmed) so the spec no longer deletes real registered repos in `beforeEach`. `diff.spec.ts` now **3/3**.

The security invariant (args array, never a shell, paths after `--`) was always upheld; `spawn` is used deliberately for the live `ChildProcess` handle the `AbortSignal`/timeout cancellation needs. The same wording still appears in historical `docs/plans`/`docs/prompts` and the architecture diagram — left as point-in-time records; sweep separately if wanted.

### 2026-07-12 — Phase 99: Secret scan runs at the commit gate (core + main + IPC + renderer)

- Built: `checkCommit` (`SafetyCheckService.ts`) gained an optional `stagedDiffs?: StagedDiff[]` input (`StagedDiff { path, diff }`, new shared type in `types.ts`); a new pure `collectStagedSecretIssues` helper reuses the EXISTING deterministic scanner rather than a parallel pattern table — it extracts added lines via `extractAddedDiffLines` (newly exported from `changeReview.ts`, previously private) and runs `findSecretMatches` (`redaction.ts`, unchanged) over them, emitting one `STAGED_SECRET_DETECTED` blocker per offending file via the already-catalogued `stagedSecretMessage(path)`. This cross-import (`src/core/safety/` → `src/core/ai/`) follows the exact precedent `remediation.ts` already set by importing `SAFETY_ACTION_BY_CODE` from `safetyCopilotMessages.ts` — both stay inside `src/core/`. Main: new `GitService.getStagedDiffs(repoPath)` — no new git plumbing, it calls the existing `getStatus` to find staged paths (same filter `hasStagedChanges` already uses elsewhere) then the existing `getDiff(repoPath, path, true)` per file, so every git invocation in the new path was already reviewed in prior phases. New `git:getStagedDiffs` IPC channel reuses the existing `GitRepoPathPayload` Zod schema (no new schema needed — it takes only `repoPath`) plus the standard preload/`window.d.ts` bridge additions. Renderer: `commitStore` fetches staged diffs in the same `Promise.all` as `status`/`identity` on `load()` (a channel failure silently yields `[]` rather than surfacing as a load error — the scan is defense-in-depth, not core to loading the screen), clears `stagedDiffs` to `[]` after a successful `doCommit` (a commit always empties the staged set), and `CommitScreen` passes the store's `stagedDiffs` into `checkCommit`. No new UI surface: the blocker renders through the existing severity-grouped issues list; its `review-staged-changes` remediation already exists and is filtered out on the Commit screen only because it points back to "commit" (the screen the user is already on) — the issue message alone (naming the file) is the fix instruction.

Safety-reviewer confirmed no `console`/`Logger` call anywhere in the new path despite diff text now flowing main→renderer (intentional — the renderer needs the raw staged diff to run the scanner client-side), the new IPC payload is Zod-validated via the reused schema, and the whole operation is read-only/non-destructive/non-remote (rule #6 doesn't apply).

### 2026-07-12 — Phase 100: Push verifies outgoing authorship + truthful push-identity copy (core + main + IPC + renderer + e2e)

Renderer: `RemoteScreen`'s push sheet fetches the outgoing range for ANY remote (not just HTTPS GitHub) when it opens, gated by a new `outgoingCommitsPending` flag that — like the existing `pushStatusPending` GitHub-token guard — blocks the Confirm button, blocks the success banner, AND is threaded into `checkPush` as `undefined` while pending, so a fetch-in-flight can never silently read as "nothing to check, so it's clean." `aiChatStore`'s bare `/explain` gets the same input (resolved via the existing pure `resolvePushTarget` helper), so the chat summary and the push sheet can't disagree. **Copy-truth fixes:** the push-sheet's "✓ Identity check passed — safe to push." became `STR.PUSH_SAFE_TO_PUSH(githubChecked)`, which only claims the GitHub-account check ran when a GitHub context genuinely exists for this push, and additionally only renders once BOTH pending flags have cleared (this also incidentally closes a pre-existing, unrelated gap where the banner could theoretically render mid-GitHub-token-fetch). `GITHUB_ACCOUNT_MISMATCH`'s message now names the real source of the expected login — `GitHubPushContext` gained `assignedLoginSource?: 'policy' | 'profile'`, and a new `githubAccountMismatchMessage(source)` picks the right wording (falls back to the original "assigned profile" text when the source is `'profile'`/omitted, so no caller needed updating).

### 2026-07-12 — Phase 101: Rename-proof `.git` watching + `config` coverage (main + renderer)

- Built: Replaced the two per-file `fs.watch` calls on `.git/HEAD` and `.git/index` with ONE non-recursive `fs.watch` on the `.git` DIRECTORY itself (`RepoWatcherService.ts`'s new `watchGitDirTopLevel`), filtering the reported `filename`: `'HEAD'` → `'head'`, `'index'` → `'index'`, `'config'` → new `'config'` kind, `'packed-refs'` → `'refs'` (anything else silently ignored). This is rename-proof by construction: git rewrites both HEAD and the index via a `*.lock`-then-`rename()` pattern, and a per-file watch follows the ORIGINAL inode — once git's rename replaces it, the watch is silently dead after its first event (the exact bug the 12 Jul QA run reproduced: three rewrites → one event). A directory watch tracks entries BY NAME instead, so a rename-in is reported like any other change, surviving every subsequent external switch/commit/config-edit indefinitely, not just the first. The existing recursive `refs/` watch (nested branch names) and the 400ms debounce are unchanged. `RepoChangeKind` gained `'config'` end-to-end (the Zod enum in `ipc-schemas.ts`, the preload/`window.d.ts` event-type mirrors). Renderer routing needed ZERO logic changes: `App.tsx`'s existing `event.kind === 'index' ? 'index' : 'full'` ternary already routes `'config'` into the `'full'` refresh (branch list + header guard + active screen) since `'config' !== 'index'` — only its comment was updated to document why that's the correct, intentional route (a `.git/config` edit needs the header guard's identity re-check just as much as a branch move does).
- Post-review hardening: none needed — safety-reviewer confirmed the diff clean on first pass. Specifically verified: `watchGitDirTopLevel` never reads file CONTENTS (only the bare `filename` from `fs.watch`'s callback), so a `.git/config` edit can never leak credential-shaped content even though the file itself is now watched; `RepoChangedEventPayload.parse(...)` still validates every emitted kind (including the new `'config'` value) before `sender.send`; no new git/child_process invocation was introduced anywhere (pure `fs.watch`/`fs.readdirSync`/`fs.statSync`); and the `switch(filename)`'s explicit allowlist (only `HEAD`/`index`/`config`/`packed-refs` match, `default: break`) means git-internal lock/temp files (`HEAD.lock`, `COMMIT_EDITMSG`, `FETCH_HEAD`, etc.) are silently ignored at the source, not merely absorbed by debounce.
- Files: edited `src/main/services/RepoWatcherService.ts` (`watchGitDirTopLevel` replaces the two `watchFileIfExists` calls; `RepoChangeKind` gains `'config'`), `src/main/ipc/ipc-schemas.ts` (`RepoChangedEventPayload.kind` enum), `preload/index.ts`, `src/renderer/types/window.d.ts` (`RepoChangedEvent.kind` type), `src/renderer/App.tsx` (comment only — no logic change); extended `tests/unit/repo-watcher-service.test.ts` with 3 new tests: the QA minimal repro as a regression test (three atomic `HEAD.lock`-then-rename rewrites → three `'head'` events, spaced past the debounce window — the old file-watch produced only one), three real `git switch`es between existing branches each firing `'head'`, and a `git config user.email` edit firing `'config'`.
- Tests: Vitest **1027/1027 passed** (114 files; 3 new tests) — the watcher test file re-run 3× to rule out `fs.watch` timing flakiness, all green.
- Exit criteria: ✅ met — `npx tsc --noEmit` clean on both tsconfigs; `npm test` green (1027/1027) including the rename-proof watcher tests; `npm run lint` clean; no watcher leaks (the pre-existing "unwatch stops further events" and "watching a different repo closes the previous watch" tests already cover the new directory watch, since `watchGitDirTopLevel`'s returned `Closeable.close()` is collected into the same `state.watchers` array `unwatch()` already iterates).

Next: **Phase 102 — Operation outcomes survive refreshes + success confirmations (renderer)** — splitting "operation outcome" state from "loaded data" state so a watcher-triggered refresh (now rename-proof and firing on every external change, thanks to this phase) stops wiping the very banner an operation just posted.

### 2026-07-12 — Phase 102: Operation outcomes survive refreshes + success confirmations (renderer)

- Built: Chose the lighter design named in the plan's own open question — per-store fields with repo-keyed reset, not one shared outcome slice (each store already tracks its own outcome fields; centralizing would mean new plumbing for zero benefit). In `remoteStore.ts`/`branchStore.ts`/`commitStore.ts`/`historyStore.ts`, `load()` now derives `isRepoChange = get().repoPath !== repoPath` and only resets the outcome fields (`successMessage`/`lastFailure` in remoteStore; `successMessage`/`mergeConflict`/`switchError`/`stashPopConflict` in branchStore; `committedHash` in commitStore; `returnSuccessMessage` in historyStore) when it's `true` — a same-repo refresh (the Phase 96/101 watcher, focus revalidation, a redundant repo re-select) no longer wipes them, closing the deterministic worst case: a failed pull's own fetch phase moves refs → the watcher fires ~400ms later → the OLD code wiped the banner it had just posted, every single time, on the very first attempt. Branch-store's confirm-armed fields (`deleteConfirmBranch`/`forceDeleteConfirmBranch`/`mergeConfirmBranch`) keep resetting UNCONDITIONALLY on every `load()` — they're not outcomes, and the plan's own file list only names the four outcome fields. Deleted `remoteStore.doFetch`'s now-unneeded ordering workaround (the success message no longer needs to be set AFTER the reload calls to survive them). **New `stashPopConflict` field (branchStore)**, split out of `switchError`: the "Bring changes & switch" quick-fix's OWN pop-conflict failure was previously ALSO recorded as `switchError`, whose only renderer (`GlobalHeader`) offers a "Bring changes & switch" RETRY button — actively wrong for this case, since the switch itself already succeeded and only the stash restore conflicted; retrying would stash-push-switch-pop again over an already-conflicted tree. `stashRef` is hardcoded `'stash@{0}'`: this repo's writes are serialized through one compound job (`GitService.enqueueJob`), so nothing else in the app could have pushed a stash in between. `StatusScreen.tsx` renders a new persistent, dismissible banner (`STR.STASH_POP_CONFLICT_BANNER`) naming the branch, the conflict message, AND the kept stash entry — replacing the bare `!`-marked conflicted-file rows with no explanation the QA report flagged. **Success confirmations:** commit already had one (`CommitScreen`'s "✓ Committed {hash}") — it just needed the survival fix above; merge already had one too (`BranchesScreen`'s `successMessage`/`STR.BRANCH_MERGE_SUCCESS`) — same fix. Uncommit had none: `historyStore` gained `returnSuccessMessage`, set on a successful `returnLast`/`returnAllUnpushed` (naming the count for the "all" path) — since both auto-navigate to Status immediately, the confirmation's real audience is a user who returns to History afterward (an operation OUTCOME being _visible whenever you look_, not a fleeting toast), so `HistoryScreen.tsx` renders it in the same success style as Commit/Remote.

### 2026-07-12 — Phase 103: Honest failure copy + reconnect shows its code (main + renderer)

- Built: `ErrorMapper.classify` gained two new patterns. **`noCredentialsAvailable`** — `could not read Username` (no HTTPS credentials at all, since `GIT_CONFIG_NOSYSTEM=1` hides the system keychain helper by design) is now checked BEFORE `authenticationFailed` and REMOVED from that bucket's regex, so the two stay mutually exclusive: "there is nothing to reject" (honest copy: "GitWarden has no saved login for this HTTPS remote — connect GitHub for this profile to push with its token") versus "a credential was rejected" (401/403/token-invalid, unchanged). Mapped to the same `reconnect-github` remediation `authenticationFailed` already had (`remediation.ts`). **`localChangesWouldBeOverwritten`** — "Your local changes to the following files would be overwritten by (checkout|switch|merge)" previously fell all the way through to the generic "An unexpected Git error occurred"; now names the real cause with plain copy, and deliberately carries NO remediation-model entry — the existing switch-failure banner (GlobalHeader) already offers "Bring changes & switch" beside whatever message renders, so wiring it into the generic model would be redundant. Both `GitErrorCode`s also threaded through the two previously-total `Record<GitErrorCode, …>` maps in `failureExplainMessages.ts` (the AI Failure Explainer's category/action catalogues) plus new `actionHintForFailure` cases. **Reconnect visibility (the harder half):** chose option (a) from the plan's open question — keep the existing panel + device-code hint mounted through the wait, not the global `ConnectGitHubModal` (which turned out to NOT actually be globally mounted in `App.tsx` despite the plan's grounding section claiming so — it's only reachable from `ProfilesScreen`, so routing into it would have meant NEW global-modal plumbing, the opposite of "lighter"). The real bug: `RemediationButton.run()` fired `onSuccess` the INSTANT `startDeviceAuth` returned a device code — which is not success, just "waiting begins" — so a caller's `onSuccess` handler (e.g. the recovery banner's `clearMessages()` + reload) could unmount the code hint before the user had even glanced at it. Fixed with a new `useEffect` (subscribing to the EXISTING `github:authEvent` channel `ConnectGitHubModal` already uses, filtered to the target profile) that waits for the REAL `'authorized'` event before calling `onSuccess`, showing "✓ Authorized as @login" in the meantime. A terminal `denied`/`expired`/`error` event clears the code and calls `onFailure` instead.
- Post-review hardening: two real issues surfaced and fixed before this phase closed, both caught empirically (not by static review alone). (1) **The reconnect e2e test failed even though the auth event WAS received correctly** — debug tracing (temporarily) proved `setAuthorizedLogin` + `onAuthEvent`'s 'authorized' branch fired exactly as designed, but the very next line unconditionally called `onSuccess` the moment `reloadProfiles()` resolved — and since the recovery banner's `onSuccess` calls `clearMessages()` (removing `lastFailure`, which unmounts this button from the tree via `recoveryRemediation` going undefined), React never got an observable chance to paint "Authorized as @octocat" before the unmount. Real fix (not a test-only workaround): gate the `onSuccess` call behind a `AUTHORIZED_DISPLAY_MS` (1500ms) `setTimeout` so the confirmation is genuinely human-readable before anything can react to it — this is the actual acceptance criterion ("stays readable … until Authorized as @…"), not just a technically-correct state transition. (2) Safety-reviewer flagged (non-blocking) that `onSuccess`/`onFailure`/`reloadProfiles` were excluded from the effect's dependency array, so a parent re-render mid-wait (the real-world wait for authorization can be arbitrarily long, not just 1.5s) could leave the effect calling a stale closure. Fixed with the EXACT ref pattern `ConnectGitHubModal.tsx` already uses for the identical problem ("Keep the latest callback without re-subscribing the event listener") — `onSuccessRef`/`onFailureRef`/`reloadProfilesRef`, updated every render, read via `.current` inside the effect.

### 2026-07-12 — Phase 104: AI tells the truth: `/push-brief` token facts + `/propose` empty-proposal guard (renderer + main)

- Built: **`/push-brief` token facts.** `PushBriefService.buildDeterministic` previously defaulted `hasToken`/`tokenInvalid` to `false`/`false` unless the CALLER passed a `github` block — and `aiChatStore.ts`'s `/push-brief` case never did, so the brief fabricated "no stored token" even minutes after a real token push. Fix: `PushBriefService` gained a 5th constructor dependency, `github: Pick<IGitHubAuthCoordinator, 'getPushContext'>` — the SAME already-constructed coordinator instance the real push sheet uses (wired at `electron/index.ts`, passed AFTER `github` is constructed, confirmed not a used-before-declared reference). When `input.github` is absent AND the remote is HTTPS GitHub AND the repo has an assigned profile, `buildDeterministic` now calls `this.github.getPushContext(assignedProfile.id)` to resolve the REAL `hasToken`/`tokenInvalid`/`effectiveLogin` — single source by construction, so the brief can never contradict the push sheet. No renderer change was needed: `aiChatStore.ts`'s `/push-brief` call already omits `github`, which is exactly the condition that now triggers the fallback resolution. **`/propose` empty-proposal guard**, three layers: (1) the chat message for a zero-`fileEdits` proposal now says the model produced no usable edits (try rephrasing) instead of silently showing just the summary with no file list; (2) `applyProposal` refuses defensively (a plain error bubble, no IPC call) if `fileEdits.length === 0`, even though `AiChatPanel`'s Apply button (3) is now gated on `fileEdits.length > 0` and would never call it in the first place; (4) belt-and-braces, `AgenticActionExecutor.executeFileEdits` now THROWS immediately for an empty array instead of silently succeeding with `{writtenFiles: []}` — the exact "Applied 0 file edit(s)" false-success the QA report observed.
- Post-review hardening: none needed — safety-reviewer confirmed the diff clean on first pass, specifically verifying: the AI stays advisory throughout (the push-brief remains read-only; the empty-batch guard only ADDS a refusal, never a new autonomous-execution path; Apply still requires an explicit human click); `getPushContext`'s result only ever carries `hasToken`/`tokenInvalid`/`effectiveLogin` (a public username) into `formatPushIdentityNote` — the exact same non-secret fields that code already handled, just populated with real values instead of hardcoded `false`; no new IPC channel, git invocation, or `child_process` call anywhere in the diff; and the DI wiring order in `electron/index.ts` is correct (the `github` coordinator is constructed before `pushBriefService`, and it's the identical instance used elsewhere, not a second one).
- Files: edited `src/main/ai/PushBriefService.ts` (5th constructor dep + fallback resolution), `electron/index.ts` (construction-site wiring), `src/main/ai/AgenticActionExecutor.ts` (empty-batch throw), `src/renderer/store/aiChatStore.ts` (`/propose` empty-case message + `applyProposal` refusal), `src/renderer/components/AiChatPanel.tsx` (Apply button gate), `src/renderer/strings.ts` (`CHAT_PROPOSAL_EMPTY`, `CHAT_PROPOSAL_EMPTY_REFUSAL`); new `tests/unit/push-brief-service.test.ts` (5 tests: resolves real facts when the caller omits `github`, reports false honestly — not a stale default, skips the lookup when the caller already supplied facts / the remote isn't HTTPS GitHub / the repo has no assigned profile); updated `tests/unit/ai-chat-store-origin-pin.test.ts` (2 new tests: the empty-proposal chat message, `applyProposal`'s defensive refusal), `tests/unit/agentic-action-executor.test.ts` (1 new test: the empty-batch throw).
- Tests: Vitest **1060/1060 passed** (117 files; 8 net-new over Phase 103's 1052). `npm run eval` **5/5 passed** (this phase touched `src/main/ai/**`, triggering the run-track EVAL step). Regression-checked `ai-chat-panel.spec.ts` (6/6) and `ai-origin-pin.spec.ts` (3/3) — 9/9 e2e passed, confirming the existing fake-AI-adapter flows (which always return non-empty `fileEdits`) are unaffected.
- Exit criteria: ✅ met — `npx tsc --noEmit` clean on both tsconfigs; `npm test` green (1060/1060) including the new chat/store/service tests; safety-reviewer confirmed clean; `npm run lint` clean.
- Notes / follow-ups: This is the last phase before **Phase 105 — Polish batch + crash telemetry + regression sweep — the track's feature-complete stop point**, per the plan's own explicit flag. Per the run-track skill's rule, the loop continues into 105 (not a stop point itself) but STOPS after 105's commit regardless of any phases remaining — there are none; 105 is also the plan's last phase.

### 2026-07-12 — Phase 105: Polish batch + crash telemetry + regression sweep (renderer + main + e2e)

- Built: six small, independent UI/UX fixes plus the track's full regression sweep. **Live theme preview** — extracted `applyTheme` out of `App.tsx` into a new `src/renderer/theme.ts` (avoids a circular import, since `SettingsScreen` needs it too); `SettingsScreen`'s appearance picker now calls it immediately on selection (before Save), with a cleanup-only `useEffect` that reverts to the persisted appearance if the user navigates away without saving. **Enter sends a fully-typed slash command** — `AiChatPanel.tsx`'s `handleMenuKeyDown` now special-cases an exact command match on Enter (not Tab) to send instead of letting the popup re-complete a command that has nothing left to complete. **Tooltip anchor tracking** — `TooltipLayer.tsx` gained a poll (every 200ms, only while a tooltip is showing) that hides the tooltip if its anchor disconnects from the DOM, or refreshes its captured rect if the anchor moved; in practice Chromium's own hit-test recompute already catches most such changes via the existing `pointerover` "nothing here" branch (verified empirically — a reflow or unmount test still passed with the poll effect disabled), but that's incidental browser behavior, not a guarantee, so the explicit poll is real defense-in-depth rather than relying on it. **Staged-deletion icon** — extracted the per-status badge (color + one-letter abbreviation) already used by `StatusScreen.tsx` into a new shared `src/renderer/components/FileStatusBadge.tsx`; `CommitScreen.tsx`'s staged-changes summary now renders one per file (keyed off `indexStatus`) instead of a hardcoded green `+` for every row regardless of add/modify/delete/rename. **Uncommit panel label** — `HistoryScreen.tsx`'s return-to-working-changes panel gained a title (`STR.HISTORY_RETURN_PANEL_TITLE`, "Undo a commit (keeps your changes)") above its actions/refusal text, which QA found floating unlabeled; the eligibility-staleness half of the plan's phrasing turned out to already be correctly handled (`historyStore.load()`'s request-guard + repo/branch-change gating, `refreshActiveRepo`'s full-refresh-on-`history`-screen wiring — all pre-existing from Phases 89/95/96/102), confirmed by reading the call graph rather than assumed. **Distinct email validation** — `ProfilesScreen.tsx`'s save handler now checks a filled-but-malformed email against a plain regex before the generic required-fields check, with its own message (`STR.PROFILE_EMAIL_INVALID`, "Enter a valid email address") instead of reusing "are required" for a non-empty field. **De-jargoned policy labels** — `PUSH_POLICY_MODE_UNRESTRICTED`/`PUSH_POLICY_MODE_BRANCH_SCOPED` reworded to "All branches allowed (except protected ones)" / "Only listed branches allowed", matching the plain-language "Protected branch patterns" field already below them. **Crash telemetry** — `electron/index.ts` now subscribes to `render-process-gone` (per-window) and `child-process-gone` (app-level), logging `reason`/`exitCode`/`type` via the existing `Logger` — closing the QA report's observability gap around an unconfirmed app exit, without a confirmed root cause to fix (nothing reproduced).
- Full regression sweep: ran the complete Vitest suite, `npm run lint`, and the complete Playwright suite in 5 chunks (the full run exceeds 10 minutes). Found and fixed two **pre-existing, unrelated** e2e failures surfaced by the sweep (present on `main` before this phase, confirmed by re-running against a stash of this phase's changes): `tests/e2e/ai-change-review.spec.ts`'s two tests asserting "staged secret-like content does not block commit" were testing behavior Phase 99 intentionally changed (secrets now block commit deterministically, independent of AI) — updated both to assert the new, correct behavior (`commit-blocker` visible, `commit-btn` disabled) instead of weakening Phase 99's gate. A third test (`ai-connections.spec.ts`'s "paste key → save" test) flaked once in the full run and passed cleanly in isolation and on retry — a pre-existing timing sensitivity, not a regression. Re-verified all 9 QA-report "Broken" items plus the relevant "Confusing" UX notes against `docs/investigations/qa-run-2026-07-12.md`'s original repros and annotated each with its fixing phase (or "still open" for the 4 items outside this track's explicit scope: no-clone-from-URL, the conflict-display double-link UX note, the post-Initialize landing screen, and the `commit-needs-log.sh` DX note).
- Post-review hardening: safety-reviewer reviewed the diff focused on `electron/index.ts`'s new crash-telemetry listeners (the only main-process surface touched this phase) — confirmed both handlers log only enum-like `reason`/`exitCode`/`type` fields from Electron's own event payload (never user/app data), routed through the existing `Logger`'s redaction rather than bypassing it via a raw `console.log`. No `src/core/` files were touched, so core-purity-reviewer's trigger rule didn't apply.

Full details in `CHANGELOG.md`. Tests: Vitest 1060/1060, full Playwright suite green.

### 2026-07-12 — Windows CI fixes: autocrlf mismatch + unhandled watcher errors

The v0.5.0 tag's Windows build failed `npm test`. Root-caused via a temporary diagnostic branch (`debug/windows-stash-repro`, deleted) that ran the failing scenario directly on Windows CI with verbose state dumps — not reproducible by local reasoning alone, since it depended on the real Windows runner's system git config.

- **`core.autocrlf` mismatch (real bug, not test-only):** `GitRunner` sets `GIT_CONFIG_NOSYSTEM=1` on every invocation (SECURITY.md #3 — never trust the system-level gitconfig). A stock Git for Windows install puts `core.autocrlf=true` in exactly that system file, so `GitRunner`'s own view of line-ending normalization silently diverged from the user's own terminal — confirmed on real Windows CI to genuinely stash-and-conflict an untouched file in `stashSwitchPop`. This affects the shipped app on any Windows machine with the standard Git for Windows install, not just this test. Fixed: `GitRunner.execute()` now injects `-c core.autocrlf=true` via a new `buildArgs()`, win32 only — restoring just this one value explicitly (the highest-precedence override) rather than trusting the untrusted system file wholesale. New tests in `tests/integration/git-runner.test.ts` (simulates `process.platform` for both branches). One pre-existing test assertion in `tests/unit/git-service-stash-switch-pop.test.ts` needed to become line-ending-tolerant, since a stash-restore now legitimately smudges to CRLF on Windows — a platform-correct difference, not a regression.
- **Unhandled `fs.watch` error events (real crash risk, not just a test flake):** the same Windows run's `repo-watcher-service.test.ts` left an uncaught `EPERM: operation not permitted, watch` even with every test passing. `RepoWatcherService`'s directory watchers (`watchGitDirTopLevel`, `watchRefsDir`) never attached an `'error'` listener; Node throws when an EventEmitter's `'error'` event has no listener, and Windows emits exactly that when a watched directory disappears while still being watched — which a real user deleting/moving a repo folder while GitWarden is watching it would also trigger, not just test cleanup. Fixed: both watchers get a no-op `.on('error', ...)`, matching the file's existing setup-time `catch {}` philosophy. New test spies on `fs.watch` to capture the real watcher and directly emits `'error'` on it, asserting no throw.
- Files: `src/main/git/GitRunner.ts`, `tests/integration/git-runner.test.ts`, `tests/unit/git-service-stash-switch-pop.test.ts`, `src/main/services/RepoWatcherService.ts`, `tests/unit/repo-watcher-service.test.ts`.
- Verification: both fixes confirmed together on real Windows CI (117/117 files, 1061/1063 tests, 2 skipped, zero uncaught exceptions) before landing on `main`. Locally: Vitest 1063/1063 (117 files), both tsconfigs clean, lint clean.
- Not yet released: v0.5.0 is already tagged and pushed without this fix. Awaiting a decision on whether to move the existing tag or cut v0.5.1.

### 2026-07-13 — v0.5.1 CI: Windows fixed, Ubuntu caught a test bug

The v0.5.1 release build confirmed the fix: Windows job green (5m47s), macOS green, both published their installers to the v0.5.1 draft on `gitwarden-releases`. Ubuntu failed, but on the NEW `repo-watcher-service.test.ts` test added for this same release, not a product regression — it asserted an exact sync-throw from `watcher.emit('error', ...)`, which depends on `fs.watch`'s platform-specific backend (inotify/FSEvents/ReadDirectoryChangesW) in ways that don't hold uniformly. Rewrote the test to assert the code calls `.on('error', ...)` on whichever watcher(s) actually get created, rather than depending on exact EventEmitter throw-timing semantics. Verified locally: Vitest 1063/1063 (117 files), both tsconfigs clean, lint clean. v0.5.1's draft is currently missing only the Linux artifact (blocked by this same test failure) — nothing has been promoted/published, so re-triggering the same tag once this commit lands is safe.

### 2026-07-13 — v0.5.1 CI, take 2: same spy-on-real-FSWatcher fragility

Moved the v0.5.1 tag to the previous commit and re-triggered: macOS green again, Ubuntu failed AGAIN on the same test — this time `vi.spyOn(watcher, 'on')` recorded zero calls on Linux even though the service demonstrably calls `.on('error', ...)` (same source on every platform). Two different real-`FSWatcher`-introspection strategies both proved unreliable across platforms — a sign the *testing approach* (spying on a real, native-backed `FSWatcher`) is the fragile part, not the fix. Rewrote the test to mock `fs.watch()` into returning a fully-controlled fake (`class FakeWatcher extends EventEmitter`), so the assertion exercises pure, platform-independent `EventEmitter` semantics instead of any native `fs.watch` backend (inotify/FSEvents/ReadDirectoryChangesW) internals. Verified locally 5× stable, full suite green (1063/1063, 117 files), both tsconfigs and lint clean. Re-moved v0.5.1's tag again to this commit.
